### 6. Unify
**Website:** [unifygtm.com](https://www.unifygtm.com) | **Founded / HQ:** 2023 ([Built In profile, third-party](https://builtin.com/job/software-engineer-ai/7563614)) / offices in San Francisco and New York ([Ashby board](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/unify)); Built In lists Munich as HQ, which conflicts with every first-party source and is probably wrong | **Category:** AI-native outbound platform: agent, B2B data marketplace, signals, plays, sequencing and deliverability

**Differentiator:** Unify treats data as a vetted wholesale marketplace (40+ named vendors, provider shown per data point, cost passed through in credits) and wraps it in "plays": a visual trigger and action graph where an AI research agent qualifies each record before it is prospected, enrolled and synced to the CRM. Since June 2026 it also exposes the whole agent to Claude and ChatGPT as a plugin, so the seller can run outbound from a general assistant. It publishes more about its evaluation method than most peers.

**Source coverage:** Website and product pages: read ([signals](https://www.unifygtm.com/products/signals), [home](https://www.unifygtm.com)) | Pricing: read ([pricing](https://www.unifygtm.com/pricing)) | Docs: read, 1 page in full plus index ([build a play](https://docs.unifygtm.com/reference/plays/building-a-play)) | Changelog: read ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)) | Blog and engineering blog: read, 4 posts in full, others search extract only ([evals](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-we-build-evals-for-ai-agents), [plugins](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/bringing-the-unify-agent-to-claude-and-chatgpt), [Next Chapter](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter)) | Case studies: read in pass 1, index re-checked ([Perplexity](https://www.unifygtm.com/customers/perplexity)) | Video and webinars: search extract only (no YouTube channel found; live demo event page, [event](https://www.unifygtm.com/events/unifys-next-chapter)) | Founder posts and podcasts: not found | Funding and launch: read ([Series B](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/series-b)) | Jobs: read, 11 open roles plus 1 removed AI posting ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/unify), [AI engineer](https://builtin.com/job/software-engineer-ai/7563614)) | Integrations: read via signals vendor list and changelog | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- Funding history: $6.6M seed led by OpenAI Startup Fund, Thrive Capital and Emergence; $12M Series A; $40M Series B led by Battery Ventures in July 2025 ([Unify blog index](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter); [BusinessWire](https://businesswire.com/news/home/20250714813159/en/Unify-Raises-$40-Million-Series-B-to-Transform-Go-To-Market-with-AI)).
- Published case studies include Perplexity, Juicebox, Campfire, Spellbook, Pylon, HyperComply, Navattic, Justworks and Flock Safety ([changelog footer](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
- High shipping cadence: 187 features in its 2025 product year (vendor figure, [This Year in Product, search extract](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/this-year-in-product)), consistent with the [changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog).
- 11 open roles on Ashby today, 7 of them engineering (Applied AI Engineer, product and platform engineers), plus AE, New Business Rep and SDR ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/unify)).

**Vendor claims**
- 8x revenue growth year on year at Series B ([BusinessWire](https://businesswire.com/news/home/20250714813159/en/Unify-Raises-$40-Million-Series-B-to-Transform-Go-To-Market-with-AI)).
- Private beta of the June 2026 agent relaunch: 57,548 queries completed, growing 45% week on week ([Next Chapter](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter)).
- "Up to 90% time savings on list building and 19% higher output per rep" ([job posting, third-party board](https://builtin.com/job/software-engineer-ai/7563614)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- Outcome language is speed and reply rate: "90% faster" list to sequence, 2.5x reply rate, 40% of pipeline from signals (vendor claim, [home](https://www.unifygtm.com)).
- Signal research is used as proof: signal-driven outbound is replied to 73% more often than cold, and reply rates double when four or more signals are stacked (vendor claim citing "proprietary data and research (2026)", [signals](https://www.unifygtm.com/products/signals)). Product usage signals show a 9.1% positive reply rate, "the highest-performing signal type" (vendor claim, [blog metadata, search extract](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/your-warmest-leads-are-already-using-your-product)).
- Case study: Perplexity booked 80+ enterprise meetings and $1.7M pipeline in three months without a BDR (vendor case study, [blog](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-perplexity-booked-1-7m-in-pipeline-without-a-single-bdr)).
- Pricing is seats plus credits, not outcomes. Free (GPT 5.4 "standard models", 1 mailbox); Base $20/seat/month with 800 credits; Pro $60/seat/month with 2,400 credits and read-only CRM sync; Business custom with first-party website intent, product signals, signal-triggered automated plays, managed mailboxes at $25/mailbox/month and read-write CRM sync. "Most actions require 1-2 credits"; LLM cost is passed through in credits with no separate AI fee ([pricing](https://www.unifygtm.com/pricing)). Automation, the part that runs without a rep, is gated to the Business tier.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Ingest data and signals.** 1.1B+ contacts and 65M+ companies, 7+ contact data waterfalls (Wiza, Forager, Prospeo, ContactOut, Findymail) and 40+ signal partners such as Store Leads, Openmart, The Swarm, BuiltWith, BuyerCaddy and PredictLeads ([Next Chapter](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter)). The signals page lists 18 categories, from hiring and funding to web intent, SEO traffic, ecommerce and network relationships ([signals](https://www.unifygtm.com/products/signals)). First-party product events arrive via PostHog, Segment or the Unify Intent Client, and warehouse data via the Unify API ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
2. **Define the audience.** Audiences are reusable filter sets (revamped filter builder, April 2026), lookalikes from seed customers via Ocean.io with a relevance score, and guided exclusions that "productized the exact ritual" Unify's own team uses with new users ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
3. **Trigger a play.** A play starts on "record enters an audience", "record matches criteria" or manual send ([docs](https://docs.unifygtm.com/reference/plays/building-a-play)).
4. **Qualify with a research agent.** The "agent qualification" action answers qualifying questions about a company or person; downstream branches act on the answer ([docs](https://docs.unifygtm.com/reference/plays/building-a-play)). Custom AI research prompts can be defined per organisation ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
5. **Prospect and enrich.** "Prospect for new people" finds contacts matching personas with a cap per company; contact details run through the waterfall ([docs](https://docs.unifygtm.com/reference/plays/building-a-play)).
6. **Write and enrol.** Agent outputs become snippet variables in email copy; sequences are routed to mailboxes by persona or CRM record owner; A/B and if/else paths test variants ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
7. **Sync and act.** Records are written to Salesforce or HubSpot, owners assigned by round robin, and webhook actions push data to n8n, Zapier or diallers ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
8. **Handle replies.** Replies are classified; out-of-office replies pause the sequence and resume on the return date, and are removed from reply metrics ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).
9. **Human in the loop.** In the chat agent the rep reviews the list and personalised copy before launch ("in a simple UX for you to review", [Next Chapter](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter)); one-off tasks and manual steps let reps intervene without enrolling ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Published eval framework.** Single-turn reply classification is scored on a human-labelled set of real email chains, using weighted accuracy that up-weights actionable tags ("needs more information", "is out of office", "already has solution") plus tone accuracy. Multi-turn research agents are scored on plan quality (human plus LLM-as-judge), tool choice and diversity, efficiency (steps), reliability (standard deviation over 5 runs) and generalisability. Eval categories: firmographics, technographics, account qualification (growth, hiring, news, social, entity recognition), business understanding and writing, each rubric scored 1 to 10 by a judge model ([evals post](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-we-build-evals-for-ai-agents)).
- **Model swaps driven by evals.** They replaced o1 with gpt-4.1 in the agent after evals showed better plans with lower cost and latency, and found o3 best for business-understanding reports ([evals post](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-we-build-evals-for-ai-agents)). GPT-5 powers AI research (August 2025) and pricing now references GPT 5.4 ([changelog](https://www.unifygtm.com/changelog); [pricing](https://www.unifygtm.com/pricing)). The agent has web search, scraping, HTML retrieval, people datasets and a browser; an OpenAI computer-using agent integration was announced ([evals post](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-we-build-evals-for-ai-agents); [blog index](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/announcing-unifys-next-chapter)).
- **Agent inside other agents.** Claude or ChatGPT calls a `run_agent` tool that starts a headless Unify session; the client then polls, fast at first and slower later, because jobs can take "tens of minutes". Bundled skills teach the client how to run the loop. Unify plans to move to MCP's Tasks extension when clients support it ([plugins post, 15 October 2026](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/bringing-the-unify-agent-to-claude-and-chatgpt)).
- **Observability per record.** Play logs show the exact path each record took and the result of every node; per-action metrics show how many records an agent qualified and why ([docs](https://docs.unifygtm.com/reference/plays/building-a-play)).
- **Stack.** TypeScript, PostgreSQL, Kubernetes, Vertica ([job listing, third-party board](https://www.wearedevelopers.com/jobs/ext/7063648/staff-software-engineer-product)); Temporal for durable backend workflows ([blog title and summary, search extract](https://www.unifygtm.com/blog/how-unify-uses-temporal)). The AI role covered agents, retrieval, classification, fine-tuning, reinforcement learning and inference infrastructure, and early access to OpenAI models ([AI engineer posting](https://builtin.com/job/software-engineer-ai/7563614)).
- Account scoring model and identity resolution method: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Breadth without owning data.** Aggregating specialist vendors and showing which vendor supplied each field gives coverage and trust quickly; credits pass cost through ([pricing FAQ](https://www.unifygtm.com/pricing)).
- **Signals tied directly to action.** The play graph removes the gap between "we saw a signal" and "someone was emailed", which is where most signal programmes fail (Inference).
- **Engineering discipline on AI quality.** Eval-led model choice keeps cost and reliability under control at volume (Inference, from the evals post).
- **Distribution** into fast-growing AI startups, a bottom-up $20 tier and a plugin inside tools reps already use (Inference). Their signal advantage relies on website and product data, which transfers poorly to industrial sellers without a product-led motion (Inference).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** count distinct signal types per account in a window and make "stacked signals" a scoring feature and a visible badge; Unify's claim is that replies double at four or more.
- **Qualified lead identification:** add an "agent qualification" step with three to five yes/no questions per ICP (for example "operates a plant over N sites?") whose answers and evidence are stored and can drive knock-outs.
- **Segmentation:** ship guided exclusion setup (competitors, current customers, open opportunities, abandoned segments) as a first-run checklist, as Unify productised its own onboarding ritual.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** express each recommendation as trigger, audience rule, qualification step and sequence, with per-account logs showing which path it took and why.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** build a reply classifier with actionable tags (out of office, needs info, has solution, referral) and score it on weighted accuracy against a small hand-labelled set from the pilot.
- **Content creation:** use research outputs as named variables in templates rather than free-form generation, so reviewers can see which fact drove which line.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 7. Lightfield
**Website:** [lightfield.com](https://lightfield.com) (lightfield.app now redirects here) | **Founded / HQ:** product launched November 2025 by Keith Peiris (CEO) and Henri Liriani (President), previously founders of Tome ([Contrary Research, third-party](https://research.contrary.com/report/lightfield)) / San Francisco with a Cambridge, MA engineering location ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/lightfield)) | **Category:** AI-native CRM built on an interaction event stream, with agentic pipeline generation

**Differentiator:** Lightfield makes captured conversations (email, calendar, meetings, Slack) the primary record and derives CRM objects from them, then lets an agent write and run Python over that history. Prospecting starts from patterns in the customer's own won, lost and stalled deals, not from a third-party database filter. Playbooks are editable skill files, so the agent's judgement is inspectable.

**Source coverage:** Website and product pages: read ([home](https://lightfield.com/)) | Pricing: read ([pricing](https://lightfield.com/pricing)) | Docs and help centre: read in pass 1 ([support](https://support.lightfield.app/articles/9385442973-how-is-lightfield-different-from-traditional-crms)) | Changelog: not found (/changelog returns 404; release notes are blog posts, [GA post, search extract](https://lightfield.app/blog/suggested-record-updates-support-in-lightfield)) | Blog: read, 3 posts ([code execution](https://lightfield.com/blog/code-execution-in-lightfield), [outbound](https://lightfield.com/blog/designing-for-outbound-that-compounds-on-itself), [Series A](https://lightfield.com/blog/our-series-a)) | Case studies: search extract only (names on home) | Video and talks: read, SaaStr live demo write-up ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)) | Founder podcasts: search extract only ([a16z podcast](https://a16z.com/podcast/the-ai-native-crm/)) | Funding: read ([a16z](https://a16z.com/announcement/investing-in-lightfield/)) | Jobs: read, 26 open roles plus 1 full posting ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/lightfield), [posting](https://jobs.a16z.com/jobs/lightfield/913e3f3a-6c16-44a7-ba7f-09159f9df620--software-engineer-agentic-pipeline-generation-staff)) | Integrations: read on home (Salesforce and HubSpot two-way sync, API, MCP, CLI) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- $47M Series A led by Andreessen Horowitz, announced 9 September 2026 ([Lightfield](https://lightfield.com/blog/our-series-a); [a16z](https://a16z.com/announcement/investing-in-lightfield/)); other investors reported as Coatue, Greylock, Lightspeed and others ([Dealroom, third-party](https://dealroom.co/news/149823-ai-crm-startup-lightfield-raises-47m-series-a-led-by-a16z/)).
- 26 open roles today, including Applied Researcher, Machine Learning Engineer and Research Scientist (all Staff+), a dedicated "Agentic Pipeline Generation" engineer, Data Infrastructure, Head of Content and a Deployment Strategist ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/lightfield)). Hiring researchers is unusual for a CRM of this age and signals investment in proprietary modelling (Inference).
- Named customer stories: SF Compute, Macroscope, Power, Intent HQ, Agree, Verdigris ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).

**Vendor claims**
- 5,000+ companies signed up since November 2025 and teams "migrating entire Salesforce implementations" ([Series A post](https://lightfield.com/blog/our-series-a)). SaaStr's June 2026 write-up gives about 3,000 customers (third-party, [SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)). The two figures measure different things (sign-ups vs customers) and neither is audited.
- 400% net dollar retention (founder-reported in coverage, third-party, [Crypto Briefing](https://cryptobriefing.com/lightfield-raises-47m-a16z-ai-crm/)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- Tool replacement and zero admin: "one bill instead of six" across CRM, call recorder, sequencer, enrichment, workflow builder and agent platform ([pricing](https://lightfield.com/pricing)).
- Outcome claims are qualitative (customer quotes); the homepage shows illustrative product screens rather than percentage uplifts ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
- Pricing (verified today): Starter $89/user/month, up to 3 seats and 50,000 records, no sequences; Pro $999/month per workspace billed annually, 5 capture seats, unlimited viewer seats, email sequences, workflows, dashboards, 250,000 records and a dedicated CSM; Growth $1,999/month, 10 capture seats, multi-channel sequences, LinkedIn sync (beta), advanced enrichment, inbox warming, outbound campaign setup and a dedicated GTM engineer; Enterprise custom with a dedicated forward-deployed engineer, bi-directional CRM sync, sandboxes and phased migration. Workspace credits for agent work are pooled, annual, do not roll over, can be hard-capped, and overage is billed at a pre-agreed rate ([pricing](https://lightfield.com/pricing)).
- Implication: outbound execution is a Growth-tier feature with human GTM engineering attached, so results are partly service-delivered (Inference).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Capture.** Connect mail, calendar, Slack, LinkedIn, a call recorder and a warehouse; in the SaaStr demo nothing was entered by hand, opportunities were created from emails and calls, and the contact book was populated from roughly ten vendors ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)).
2. **Structure.** Interactions become a "semi-structured business graph", described as "a primitive world model" of people, companies and what they said ([code execution post](https://lightfield.com/blog/code-execution-in-lightfield)). Accounts get a score plus signal chips (executive hire, headcount growth, contact job change, tech stack change, new funding) ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
3. **Form hypotheses.** Agents start from closed-won data: which accounts moved fastest, what pain they felt when found, who championed ([outbound post, 11 May 2026](https://lightfield.com/blog/designing-for-outbound-that-compounds-on-itself)).
4. **Find accounts.** An account discovery skill runs across CRM accounts not yet sold to, an ICP search runs across data sources, and unstructured notes from stalled and lost deals are read; a weekly lookalike job adds accounts ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/); [home](https://lightfield.com/)). In the demo the pattern found was buyers who responded to downtime figures and ran legacy SCADA and PLC systems, and job postings were used as a signal grade (third-party write-up).
5. **Find people.** The leader at each account is found and cross-checked across sources including LinkedIn, then contacts are created ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)).
6. **Write.** A three-step sequence is built from prior sequences, the research and the rep's writing style; messages draw on the persona's own words about pain, proof points that resonated and objections cleared ([outbound post](https://lightfield.com/blog/designing-for-outbound-that-compounds-on-itself)).
7. **Next action on live deals.** For a stalled deal the agent compares it with won and lost deals in a sandbox, finds the missing stakeholder (for example a CIO), enriches and creates the contact and drafts an email to loop them in ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)). The Deal Review skill returns "one next step, never a list" with cited evidence ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
8. **Automate.** A pattern found on one deal becomes a plain-language automation ("every time a deal reaches POC without an IT contact") that runs as Python and requests named permissions before activation ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)).
9. **Human in the loop.** Drafts are queued for review; outbound contacts sync into the CRM only once they respond, keeping the database clean ([outbound post](https://lightfield.com/blog/designing-for-outbound-that-compounds-on-itself); [SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Code execution over CRM memory.** The agent plans, writes Python, runs it in a sandbox, evaluates the result and iterates. When context is not yet structured, it dispatches sub-agents per account to read histories and interpret tone, which return structured data for the code to process at scale ([Henri Liriani, 16 February 2026](https://lightfield.com/blog/code-execution-in-lightfield)).
- **Event log, schema on top.** Emails and events are stored at the base with a CRM schema layered over them; every field, attribute and object has edit and version history ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/); [a16z podcast summary, search extract](https://a16z.com/podcast/the-ai-native-crm/)).
- **One API for agent, UI and integrations,** with rate limits and data access bound to the operator's permissions ([SaaStr](https://www.saastr.com/watching-an-ai-native-crm-run-the-full-gtm-loop-live-lightfield-ceo-keith-peiris-demos-a-stalled-deal-an-automation-and-ten-new-prospects/)). Read and write via API, MCP and CLI ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
- **Agent harness:** "a standardized SDK, deterministic code sandbox, and continuous evals" ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
- **Skills as files.** SKILL.md plus framework documents (qualification, sales process, competitors) that the agent must cite ([home](https://lightfield.com/)).
- **Compounding loop.** Sequence funnel data (goal, audience, messaging, outcome) is written to a CRM knowledge base for the next campaign ([outbound post](https://lightfield.com/blog/designing-for-outbound-that-compounds-on-itself)).
- **Stack:** TypeScript, React, Next.js, Node.js, Apollo GraphQL and PostgreSQL ([Agentic Pipeline Generation posting](https://jobs.a16z.com/jobs/lightfield/913e3f3a-6c16-44a7-ba7f-09159f9df620--software-engineer-agentic-pipeline-generation-staff)). That role is to "ingest, enrich, and connect information about companies, people, and their relationships" and build agentic workflows from research to follow-up.
- Models, scoring internals and eval metrics: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **First-party evidence.** Messages and next steps are grounded in what buyers actually said, which outbound-only tools cannot see.
- **No setup.** Auto-built records and agent-led migration remove the empty-CRM problem.
- **Inspectable reasoning.** Skill files, cited evidence and versioned fields make AI output auditable by managers (Inference).
- **Founder distribution and a16z backing.**
- **Limit:** the loop works best once a customer has a meaningful history of won and lost deals; prospect data coverage is borrowed from vendors (Inference).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Qualified lead identification:** generate ICP hypotheses from the pilot's won and lost deals (time to close, pain at first contact, champion role) and present them for approval before scoring.
- **Lead scoring:** show signal chips beside each tier, and add "stalled deal vs won deal" comparison as a diagnostic for existing pipeline.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** store the playbook as editable Markdown skill files and force one next step with cited evidence and the framework section it relies on.
- **Prospecting:** treat "missing stakeholder" (for example no plant manager or IT contact on a deal) as a trigger for finding and adding the person, highly relevant to multi-stakeholder industrial buying.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** tag each step with the objection, proof point or pain quote it uses, and log reply outcomes against those tags.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** only promote outbound contacts into the account record once they reply, to keep data clean.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 8. Nex AI
**Website:** [nex.ai](https://nex.ai/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2025 / San Francisco; Y Combinator Summer 2026 ([YC profile](https://www.ycombinator.com/companies/nex)) | **Category:** prompt-to-agent builder for GTM and RevOps, on an organisational context graph

Identification note: the homepage headline reads "Hire your first" over "AI GTM TEAM" ([home](https://nex.ai/)), not "AI GTM Engineer". The older phrase survives in the slug of the company's LinkedIn launch post linked from YC ("nexai-yc-s26-is-the-ai-gtm-engineer-that...", [YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)). YC now titles the launch "Claude Cowork for high-volume workflows, starting with GTM". Confidence that this is the intended company: high.

**Differentiator:** Nex claims a cost advantage over general agents: it has AI write code for the repetitive, deterministic parts of a GTM workflow and reserves model reasoning for the hard parts, so high-volume jobs do not burn token budgets. It packages this as 26 named role agents (Inbound SDR, Revenue Recovery, CRM Admin) that are all approval-gated, sitting on a context graph that stores decision traces and learns patterns from rep feedback.

**Source coverage:** Website and agent pages: read ([home](https://nex.ai/), [Inbound SDR template](https://nex.ai/templates/lead-scoring)) | Pricing: read ([pricing](https://nex.ai/pricing)) | Docs and API: could not access (docs.nex.ai failed DNS today; pass 1 saw it via search summary, [API reference](https://docs.nex.ai/api-reference/introduction)) | Changelog: not found | Blog: read, post index plus 1 technical post in full ([blog](https://nex.ai/blog), [context graph](https://nex.ai/blog/no-bs-guide-to-build-a-context-graph)) | Case studies: not found (one anecdote in YC launch) | Video: search extract only (launch video removed from YouTube, LinkedIn copy linked from [YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)) | Founder posts: read (YC launch post) | Funding: search extract only (investors named on home, round not published) | Jobs: not found | Integrations: read on pricing and launch (1,000+ to 1,500+); GitHub repo now 404 ([repo](https://github.com/nex-crm/nex-as-a-skill)), README mirror search extract only ([mcpservers.org](https://mcpservers.org/servers/nex-crm/nex-as-a-skill)) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- YC S26, founded 2025, team of 5 ([YC profile](https://www.ycombinator.com/companies/nex)).
- Founders: Najmuzzaman (Nazz) Mohammad (CEO), former Senior PM at HubSpot and Clipboard Health; Francisco Dias (CTO), described as a founding engineer on HubSpot CRM ([YC profile](https://www.ycombinator.com/companies/nex); [home](https://nex.ai/)).
- #1 Product of the Day on Product Hunt, 3 September 2026 (third-party, [hunted.space](https://hunted.space/dashboard/nex-4)).

**Vendor claims**
- Investors include Dharmesh Shah (HubSpot) and Girish Mathrubootham (Freshworks); round size not published ([home](https://nex.ai/)).
- "Independently audited security" ([home](https://nex.ai/)); the auditor and standard are not named.
- One customer anecdote: on a single onboarding call Nex audited thousands of HubSpot records, flagged missing data, archived owners, bad associations and "zombie deals", and started fixing them before the call ended ([YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)). No named customers or case studies found.

#### 2. How they promise results
- **Cost of work, not outcomes.** "At the price of a coffee"; work "that would burn your entire weekly limit overnight on Claude or Codex runs on autopilot" ([YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)).
- **Stack replacement per agent.** Each template shows an estimated monthly saving: Revenue Recovery $300 to $400, CRM Admin $200 to $500, Meeting Prep $100 to $200, Pipeline Manager $200 to $400, Inbound SDR $300 to $500 (vendor estimate, [home](https://nex.ai/)); pass 1 noted these are labelled as illustrative ranges ([template](https://nex.ai/templates/lead-scoring)).
- **Speed.** "Get your first agent before the call ends" in a free session with an agent expert ([home](https://nex.ai/)).
- **Pricing today:** Agent Builder Pro $49/month plus usage, with $100 of monthly AI usage included, unlimited data sources, workflows and projects, 1,500+ integrations, free trial usage and a 7-day refund ([pricing](https://nex.ai/pricing)). Pass 1 recorded a "Pay for outcomes" line for custom agents; it was not present on today's read, so outcome pricing should be treated as unconfirmed.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Describe a goal.** The user writes one prompt; Nex builds the agent from business context ("Any GTM workflow. One prompt.") or starts from one of 26 templates ([home](https://nex.ai/)).
2. **Connect context.** CRM, email, calendar, Slack, meetings and warehouse data feed the context graph (README mirror and pass 1 docs summary, [mcpservers.org](https://mcpservers.org/servers/nex-crm/nex-as-a-skill)).
3. **Find leads.** ICP List Builder, LinkedIn Lead Researcher, LinkedIn Visitor SDR and social "Buyer Scouts" on Reddit and X monitor for intent; the launch also lists scraping public records ([home](https://nex.ai/); [YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)).
4. **Score.** The Inbound SDR ingests a lead, resolves person and company, compares with closed-won evidence ("not generic MQL criteria"), scores with reasons and routes for review ([home](https://nex.ai/); [template](https://nex.ai/templates/lead-scoring)).
5. **Act.** Outbound SDR books meetings; Pipeline Manager detects silence across email, Slack and calendar and drafts follow-ups; Revenue Recovery scans closed-lost and 90+ day cold contacts, pulls past conversations, loss reasons and company changes, and drafts reconnection emails ([home](https://nex.ai/)).
6. **Execute in the browser.** "Annex", a macOS browser that agents control on the user's behalf, for tasks without APIs ([home](https://nex.ai/)).
7. **Approve.** "You approve every email"; CRM changes are proposed and applied only after approval ([home](https://nex.ai/)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Code for the deterministic, model for the judgement.** "Nex has AI write code for the predictable parts and run it fast without wasting tokens, saving AI judgment for the genuinely hard parts" ([YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm)). This mirrors Lightfield's code-execution approach but is framed as a unit-cost advantage.
- **Context graph design (published 26 January 2026).** Identity signals (emails, company names, domains, titles) are resolved against known entities; content is split into "discrete, single-thread insights" with temporal validity that supersede rather than delete; decision traces are stored as metadata on decision records; insights are embedded and clustered, with centroids becoming "pattern embeddings"; at run time the system retrieves entity context, embeds the situation, vector-searches similar patterns and injects heuristics. Rep feedback (marked helpful or ignored) updates patterns. They state that permissions for learned knowledge are unsolved ([context graph post](https://nex.ai/blog/no-bs-guide-to-build-a-context-graph)).
- **Portable context.** MCP server, Claude Code memory plugin, OpenClaw plugin, agent skill and an npm CLI with recall and capture commands ([blog index](https://nex.ai/blog); [README mirror, search extract](https://mcpservers.org/servers/nex-crm/nex-as-a-skill)). The GitHub org is named "nex-crm", suggesting an earlier CRM or memory-layer product (Inference).
- **Integrations:** Gmail, Calendar, Outlook and Slack via direct OAuth; HubSpot and Salesforce via Nango-brokered OAuth (pass 1, API docs via search summary).
- Models, LLM provider choice (FAQ answer not visible), evals and scoring internals: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- Results are not yet publicly evidenced. Inference: assets are HubSpot pedigree and investor, a familiar job-title framing for each agent, and approval-by-default that lowers trust barriers for CRM writes.
- The cost argument is credible for repetitive RevOps work (deduplication, field audits), where code is cheaper and more reliable than repeated model calls (Inference).
- The positioning has moved from context layer to "AI GTM Engineer" to "AI GTM Team" within 2026, which suggests the wedge is still being found (Inference from [blog](https://nex.ai/blog), [YC launch](https://www.ycombinator.com/launches/Sv1-nex-claude-cowork-for-high-volume-workflows-starting-with-gtm) and [home](https://nex.ai/)).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** publish our scoring pipeline as explicit steps (ingest, resolve, compare with won deals, score with reasons, route) in the UI, as Nex does per template.
- **Prospecting:** add deterministic data hygiene before scoring (duplicate, missing field, stale record audit) as a visible "CRM Admin" step; our upload validation already does part of this.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** store each human approve or ignore decision on a recommendation as a trace and use it to re-rank future recommendations.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** a "going dark" detector on silence across email and LinkedIn for accounts in active sequences.
- **Deal:** a closed-lost re-engagement play keyed on loss reason plus a new trigger (budget cycle, new site, leadership change), valuable in long industrial cycles.
- **Campaign execution:** keep model calls for judgement and write rules or code for bulk steps, to keep per-account cost predictable.

### 9. 1mind
**Website:** [1mind.com](https://www.1mind.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** founding year and HQ not stated on first-party pages reviewed; publicly launched November 2025; remote-first US hiring ([newsroom](https://www.1mind.com/announcements); [careers](https://1mind.com/careers)) | **Category:** AI "Superhumans": voice and avatar agents for website inbound, live sales calls, in-product guidance and customer success

Identification note: "Every1 Wins" is not on the homepage or careers page today ([home](https://www.1mind.com/); [careers](https://1mind.com/careers)). It appears in the 1mind YouTube channel description as indexed by a third-party analytics site: "1mind is a platform that deploys GTM Superhumans for revenue teams so that Every1 Wins, your customers and buyers, your team, and your business" ([vling channel report, third-party](https://vling.net/en/channel/UC9aLrJyOQIxkRWjuBKnc91w/channel-info)). LinkedIn could not be accessed. Company identity is not in doubt.

**Differentiator:** 1mind does not source leads; it converts demand that already exists by putting a named, voiced agent in front of buyers at the moments humans cannot cover (website, live calls as a visible sales engineer, in product). All surfaces share one memory ("1 Brain") from first visit to renewal, and it measures itself on revenue with head-to-head tests against the incumbent chatbot.

**Source coverage:** Website and use cases: read ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)) | Pricing: not found (not published) | Docs and help centre: not found (trust centre link only) | Changelog: search extract only (release notes pages, [release note](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-is-the-exclusive-ai-successor-to-drift)) | Blog: read, 1 post in full ([Salesloft integration](https://www.1mind.com/blog-articles/salesloft-integration)) | Case studies: read in pass 1 (HubSpot), Experity figures re-read in launch release | Video: search extract only (YouTube channel description via third-party, 29 videos) | Founder posts and talks: search extract only ([HumanX speaker page](https://www.humanx.co/us/speakers/amanda-kahlow); [Latka interview, third-party](https://getlatka.com/companies/1mind/interviews/1mind-amanda-kahlow-2026)) | Funding and launch: read ([newsroom](https://www.1mind.com/announcements); [Ride-Along release](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-launches-ride-along-with-the-industrys-first-ai-that-joins-live-sales-calls-as-a-visible-named-sales-engineer-speaking-directly-to-buyers)) | Jobs: read, 7 open roles plus 1 expired engineering posting ([careers](https://1mind.com/careers); [AI System Engineer, expired](https://www.theladders.com/job/ai-system-engineer-1mind-virtual-travel_84954952)) | Integrations: read (Salesloft, Clari, Zoom, Teams, Meet) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- $40M raised in total including a $30M Series A led by Battery Ventures, announced 10 November 2025 ([newsroom](https://www.1mind.com/announcements); [VCA Online, third-party](https://www.vcaonline.com/news/2025111005/1mind-publicly-launches-with-40-million-to-usher-in-new-era-of-ai-led-sales-with-highly-skilled-persuasive-and-emotionally-intelligent-superhumans-/)).
- Founder and CEO Amanda Kahlow previously founded 6sense ([HumanX speaker page](https://www.humanx.co/us/speakers/amanda-kahlow)).
- Clari + Salesloft named 1mind the exclusive AI successor to Drift, which it is sunsetting; 1mind pages show both 15 January and 6 March 2026 as dates ([newsroom](https://www.1mind.com/announcements); [release note](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-is-the-exclusive-ai-successor-to-drift)).
- Customer logos on the homepage include HubSpot, ZoomInfo, Samsara, Nutanix, Coupa, Seismic, Quickbase, Pipedrive, Experity and Alteryx ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).
- Seven open roles, all remote US, on Ashby: Forward-Deployed Engineering Lead, Prompting and Context Engineer, two AE roles, CSM and GTM enablement ([careers](https://1mind.com/careers)).

**Vendor claims**
- 78% of 1mind's own pipeline is AI-sourced; deals of $110K closed by Ride-Along "with no humans in the loop" ([Ride-Along release](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-launches-ride-along-with-the-industrys-first-ai-that-joins-live-sales-calls-as-a-visible-named-sales-engineer-speaking-directly-to-buyers)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- "Measured on revenue, not meetings booked" ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).
- Homepage figures: 16x ROI in 3 months (shown beside the ZoomInfo logo), +153% Starter purchases in 30 days (HubSpot), 77.6% win rate vs 53.6% on Drift and +145% average deal size vs Drift (Samsara) (vendor claims, [home](https://www.1mind.com/)). The HubSpot case study reports 88% visitor engagement, +78% free trial sign-ups and +25% influenced purchases, "all measured by HubSpot" (vendor case study, [HubSpot](https://www.1mind.com/case-study/hubspot)). The two HubSpot figures differ and probably measure different products or periods (Inference).
- Experity with Ride-Along: inquiry-to-opportunity 6% to 10%, MQL-to-opportunity 12% to 21%, win rate 26% to 50%, cycle 28 to 15 days, 3 closed deals per 100 inquiries vs a benchmark of 1; generic "2x to 5x conversion lift" and "62% shorter sales cycles" (vendor claims, [Ride-Along release](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-launches-ride-along-with-the-industrys-first-ai-that-joins-live-sales-calls-as-a-visible-named-sales-engineer-speaking-directly-to-buyers)).
- Pricing: not published. Third-party estimates of $100K to $400K a year come from a competitor's blog and are unverified ([Artisan, third-party](https://www.artisan.co/blog/1mind-pricing-and-reviews)).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Train the brain.** Customer content (product, pricing, technical docs, slides, demo environment) is ingested and the agent is configured for persona, voice and avatar; pass 1 found 4 to 6 weeks implementation (search extract, [use cases](https://www.1mind.com/use-cases)).
2. **Meet the buyer.** On the website, in a deal room or on video, the Superhuman "qualifies intent in real dialogue, runs interactive demos, handles objections and technical questions, builds the value case, and books the meeting" ([Salesloft post, 4 August 2026](https://www.1mind.com/blog-articles/salesloft-integration)).
3. **Join live calls.** Ride-Along enters Zoom, Teams or Meet as a named participant, in Active or Silent mode, answering technical questions, presenting slides and walking through the real UI ([Ride-Along release](https://www.1mind.com/announcements/1mind-launches-ride-along-with-the-industrys-first-ai-that-joins-live-sales-calls-as-a-visible-named-sales-engineer-speaking-directly-to-buyers)).
4. **Write back.** Buyer signals, qualification notes, intent data and AI summaries are written into Salesloft Cadence so "the discovery is already done" when the rep opens the record ([Salesloft post](https://www.1mind.com/blog-articles/salesloft-integration)).
5. **Continue post-sale.** In-product and Customer Success Superhumans share the same memory of the account ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).
6. **Human in the loop.** Hand-off to humans is a listed capability; in Silent mode Ride-Along does not speak until brought in ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Real-time multimodal pipeline.** The AI System Engineer role connected ASR, LLMs, text-to-speech and avatar rendering, with observability, tracing and evaluation frameworks for synchronisation across modalities; named stack was Python, TypeScript, Kubernetes, LangChain, Pipecat and Daily (expired listing, third-party board, [Ladders](https://www.theladders.com/job/ai-system-engineer-1mind-virtual-travel_84954952)). Pipecat and Daily are open-source and hosted real-time voice and video infrastructure, so the voice layer is assembled rather than proprietary (Inference).
- **Published design rubric.** Accuracy ("every claim traceable to a source, every number computed rather than guessed, every action validated before it executes"), breadth (present, demo, quote, schedule, query data, hand off) and latency ("inside the rhythm of human conversation"), framed as a trade-off ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).
- **Context engineering as a job.** A dedicated "Prompting and Context Engineer" and a forward-deployed engineering lead show that quality comes from per-customer configuration ([careers](https://1mind.com/careers)).
- **Data policy:** no training on customer data ([home](https://www.1mind.com/)).
- Models, retrieval design and evaluation metrics: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Wedge at the moment of intent.** They act on traffic and live deals, where impact appears within 30 days and can be A/B tested against the old chatbot.
- **Distribution.** The Drift succession hands them an installed base through Salesloft and Clari.
- **Credibility.** Kahlow's 6sense history and enterprise logos double as references.
- **High-touch deployment** produces strong agents but limits self-serve scale (Inference).
- **Relevance to Demand AI:** limited for new-logo sourcing, but strong for what happens after a prospect responds (Inference).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** when a prospect replies, generate a hand-off brief (signals, stated need, questions asked, objections) so the first human call is not another discovery call.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** use one governed knowledge base for both outreach copy and answers to replies, with every claim traceable to an approved source.
- **Campaign execution:** measure campaigns on opportunities and influenced revenue against a holdout segment, not on meetings booked.
- **Content creation:** adopt "accuracy, breadth, latency" as a review rubric; for us "accuracy" means every figure in generated content is sourced from the client's library.
- **Deal:** for high-ticket industrial deals, prepare a technical answer pack (specifications, compliance, lead times) per segment so reps can respond fast to engineering questions.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach

### 10. Monaco
**Website:** [monaco.com](https://www.monaco.com/) (page title "Monaco: The first revenue engine for startups", [search result](https://www.monaco.com/)) | **Founded / HQ:** 2025, emerged from stealth February 2026 ([yespress, third-party](https://yespress.io/monaco.md); [TechCrunch, third-party](https://techcrunch.com/2026/02/11/former-founders-fund-vc-sam-blond-launches-ai-sales-startup-to-upend-salesforce/)) / San Francisco, in-office ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco)) | **Category:** AI-native revenue platform replacing CRM and outbound tools, sold with a forward-deployed human AE

**Differentiator:** Monaco sells the motion, not a tool: the TAM is pre-built on day one, agents run demand generation inside guardrails, and every customer gets a forward-deployed AE, paid from one credit balance with no seats. Its published architecture treats memory as a write-time problem, capturing each decision with enough context to join it to outcomes later, and stores organisational memory as versioned, human-editable Markdown.

**Source coverage:** Website and product: read ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)) | Pricing: read ([pricing](https://www.monaco.com/pricing)) | Docs and API: search extract only (beta API, OpenAPI spec, MCP server, per [Supergood API report, third-party](https://supergood.ai/api-report-card/monaco)) | Changelog: not found | Engineering blog: read, 2 posts ([System of Intelligence](https://www.monaco.com/blog/a-system-of-intelligence-for-revenue), [Memory](https://www.monaco.com/blog/design-monaco-memory-for-sales-agents)) | Case studies: read, testimonials only ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)) | Video: not found | Founder posts: search extract only ([Sam Blond launch post](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/sam-blond-791026b_were-launching-monaco-today-monaco-automates-activity-7427382795578150912-W9pp)) | Funding: read via third-party hosts ([VCA Online](https://www.vcaonline.com/news/2026051211/monaco-secures-50-million-in-series-b-funding-to-grow-ai-powered-sales-platform-amid-strong-customer-demand/)) | Jobs: read, 17 open roles plus 1 full posting ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco); [AI Backend Product Engineer](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco/329d17b3-4c4c-4a22-b26e-e432ee60e86f)) | Integrations: not found (no marketplace listing seen) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- $35M at launch ($10M seed and $25M Series A, both led by Founders Fund), February 2026 ([The AI Insider, third-party](https://theaiinsider.tech/2026/02/12/monaco-emerges-from-stealth-with-35m-for-ai-native-sales-platform/)); $50M Series B led by Benchmark on 12 May 2026, total above $85M, with Founders Fund and Human Capital ([VCA Online, company release](https://www.vcaonline.com/news/2026051211/monaco-secures-50-million-in-series-b-funding-to-grow-ai-powered-sales-platform-amid-strong-customer-demand/)).
- Founders: Sam Blond (former Brex CRO, former Founders Fund partner), Abishek Viswanathan (former CPO at Apollo and Qualtrics), Malay Desai (former Clari engineering leader), Brian Blond ([The AI Insider, third-party](https://theaiinsider.tech/2026/02/12/monaco-emerges-from-stealth-with-35m-for-ai-native-sales-platform/)).
- 65 people and 17 open roles, including a Forward Deployed Engineer, two Forward-Deployed AE roles (new business and post-sales), Data Engineer, Data Platform Engineer, Solutions Engineer and Launch Operations ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco); [posting](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco/329d17b3-4c4c-4a22-b26e-e432ee60e86f)). Half the hiring is service delivery, consistent with the forward-deployed model.

**Vendor claims**
- "Hundreds of customers" during public beta ([Series B release](https://www.vcaonline.com/news/2026051211/monaco-secures-50-million-in-series-b-funding-to-grow-ai-powered-sales-platform-amid-strong-customer-demand/)); "millions in ARR within months of launch" ([posting](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco/329d17b3-4c4c-4a22-b26e-e432ee60e86f)). Business Insider, relayed by secondary outlets, reported no revenue in February and more than $1M added each month in the three months after (third-party, company-shared, unaudited, [ContentGrip](https://www.contentgrip.com/monaco-series-b/)).
- A competitor's review says general availability came on 25 August 2026 (third-party, unverified, [Salesforge](https://www.salesforge.ai/pt/blog/monaco-sales-platform-review)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- "Monaco customers grow revenue faster"; testimonials include "a 3-person team running GTM like a 20-person sales org" (Datawizz) and "TAM built on day 2 and running outbound sequences that same day" (Nowadays) ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
- Re-engagement agent runs that pick up cold conversations "sustain 20-40% response rates" (vendor claim, [System of Intelligence](https://www.monaco.com/blog/a-system-of-intelligence-for-revenue)).
- Priced on work done: $10,000 paid at signature buys 1,000,000 credits at one cent each; it covers "roughly the first three months" for most customers; then monthly billing for actual use; no seat or platform fee; no commitment beyond the first $10,000. The TAM build, signals, Demand Gen Agents and Pipeline Agents draw on one balance, with a live usage breakdown in product. A forward-deployed AE and GTM strategy support are included ([pricing](https://www.monaco.com/pricing)).
- Independent proof is thin: a competitor notes there are no hard numbers on meetings or pipeline and few G2 reviews (third-party, [Salesforge](https://www.salesforge.ai/pt/blog/monaco-sales-platform-review)).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Build the TAM.** From "a world database of billions of data points", shaped by the ICP, existing customers and accounts in the customer's email history; refreshed as the company grows ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
2. **Score.** "Built-in ML scoring using firmographics and signals with clear 'why this account' explanations" ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
3. **Overlay signals.** Semantic search (examples include "B2B companies manufacturing fasteners" and "companies hiring RAG engineers"), custom signals (common investors, job postings, tech stack) and inbound signals (website visitors, demo requests) decide "who to reach out to, when, and why" ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
4. **Respect memory.** The demand agent reads organisation memory, for example accumulated exclusions such as a competitor's parent company or an abandoned segment, and can explain why it skipped an account ([Memory post](https://www.monaco.com/blog/design-monaco-memory-for-sales-agents)).
5. **Execute.** Autopilot decides whom to enrol, when to start and how to follow up "without blasting your whole TAM"; messages adapt to business context and intent ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
6. **Capture.** Every interaction is summarised and attached to account, contact and opportunity; fields such as call count, stakeholders and "why now" are auto-filled ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
7. **Manage pipeline.** Stages move on meetings, threads and stakeholder engagement; ghosting and stalls are flagged with reasons; a CRO copilot proposes prioritised actions ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product)).
8. **Human in the loop.** Customer-set guardrails plus the forward-deployed AE ([product](https://www.monaco.com/product); [pricing](https://www.monaco.com/pricing)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Write-time capture and outcome joins.** Build order: records, identity resolution ("one buyer remained one buyer"), a shared context layer, write-time capture of actions and decisions, then joining actions to outcomes and feeding them back. Each action records the resolved person, company, sequence, message variant, segment definition and qualitative statements so later outcomes can be joined reliably. "Organizational memory is not primarily a retrieval problem. It is a write-time problem" ([System of Intelligence, 9 September 2026](https://www.monaco.com/blog/a-system-of-intelligence-for-revenue)).
- **Objection learning.** The system records objection, chosen response, subsequent movement and outcome, and across hundreds of opportunities forms judgement on which responses work per segment ([System of Intelligence](https://www.monaco.com/blog/a-system-of-intelligence-for-revenue)).
- **Monaco Memory.** Two scopes (organisation and user); versioned S3-backed Markdown; deterministic pub-sub triggers for business-context sources; a scheduled "reflection" job proposes candidate observations from chat transcripts and a later consolidation step merges them; memory is injected directly into agent context and is inspectable during evaluation ([Memory post, 30 September 2026](https://www.monaco.com/blog/design-monaco-memory-for-sales-agents)).
- **Developer surface:** markdown docs with llms.txt, an OpenAPI spec and an MCP server, all labelled beta (third-party, [Supergood](https://supergood.ai/api-report-card/monaco)).
- **Stack:** JS/TS, Go and some Python, cloud infrastructure ([posting](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/monaco/329d17b3-4c4c-4a22-b26e-e432ee60e86f)). Models, data vendors behind the world database and scoring model: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Service plus software.** The forward-deployed AE configures and runs the motion, reducing failure from poor set-up (Inference).
- **Day-one TAM** removes the empty-CRM problem.
- **Pricing that filters** for committed buyers while removing seat friction.
- **Founder networks** in Founders Fund and Benchmark portfolios provide early startup customers.
- **Architecture for learning.** If write-time capture works, the system improves with each campaign; evidence of this is not yet independent (Inference).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** attach a short "why this account" sentence to every tier, generated from the top contributing fit and signal factors.
- **Segmentation:** allow semantic segment definitions in plain language (Monaco's own example is fastener manufacturers), translated into rules the user can inspect.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** log every recommendation at write time with segment, signal, message variant and reviewer decision, so later replies and opportunities can be joined back to it.
- **Campaign execution:** cap enrolment per segment and per week and let the system pick start timing, to avoid burning a small industrial TAM.
- **Content creation:** keep organisation memory (exclusions, positioning, lessons) and user tone as versioned Markdown that users can edit and that every generation must read.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** record objection, response used and outcome as structured fields, building a per-segment objection library over the pilot.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 11. Journeys by Workvivo (Workvivo by Zoom)
**Website:** [workvivo.com/journeys](https://www.workvivo.com/journeys/) | **Founded / HQ:** Workvivo founded 2017, Cork, Ireland; acquired by Zoom in 2023 ([Futurum, third-party](https://futurumgroup.com/insights/zoom-acquires-workvivo-in-a-move-to-bolster-employee-experience)) | **Category:** Employee experience platform (intranet and employee app). Journeys is a paid add-on for automated employee lifecycle flows such as onboarding, offboarding, training and recognition. **It is not a sales tool.** It is included here only as a design reference for multi-step, triggered, personalised sequences.

**Differentiator:** Journeys treats a sequence as a first-class object with a trigger, typed steps, intervals, an audience and a completion state per person, and since July 2026 an LLM drafts the whole skeleton (trigger, steps, pacing and copy) from one prompt for an admin to approve. Its trigger data comes from systems the buyer already owns (HR provisioning files), so personalisation needs no new data collection. Compared with sales sequencers, its strongest idea is explicit acknowledgement steps that give a hard completion signal instead of opens and clicks.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read ([Journeys page](https://www.workvivo.com/journeys/)) | Pricing: search extract only, third-party ([Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/201794/Workvivo/pricing/); [Vendr](https://www.vendr.com/marketplace/workvivo)) | Docs: read, 3 articles ([triggers and steps](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31350002642077-Journeys-Triggers-and-Steps); [Journeys AI](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/38630039649565-Journeys-AI); [create and manage](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31347200668189)) | Changelog: read ([What's New](https://www.workvivo.com/new-in-product/)) | Blog: search extract only ([launch post](https://www.workvivo.com/blog/announcing-workvivo-journeys-livestreams-new-mobile-app/)) | Case studies: search extract only, none Journeys-specific ([SupportNinja](https://www.workvivo.com/case-study/supportninja/)) | Videos: not found | Leadership posts: not found for Journeys | Funding and press: search extract only (acquisition coverage) | Jobs: search extract only ([Zoom careers](https://careers.zoom.us/jobs/software-engineer-php-and-react-workvivo-ireland-remote-ireland)) | Integrations: search extract only ([HR integrations](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/7981384048541-HR-Integrations)) | Investor filings: search extract only ([Zoom FY2026 annual report letter](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001585521/000114036126018249/ef20072035_ars.pdf))

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- Zoom announced it would acquire Workvivo in April 2023; terms were not disclosed ([UC Today, third-party](https://www.uctoday.com/collaboration/zoom-to-acquire-employee-engagement-platform/)). Workvivo now sits inside the Zoom client ([UC Today, third-party](https://www.uctoday.com/collaboration/zoom-fully-integrates-workvivo-into-platform/)).
- Zoom's FY2026 shareholder letter says Workvivo customers above $100,000 ARR grew 58% across FY2026, even after the Meta Workplace transition partnership ended mid-year, and that Workvivo launched AI features powered by Zoom AI ([Zoom ARS, SEC](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001585521/000114036126018249/ef20072035_ars.pdf), search extract).
- Earnings coverage reported 1,225 Workvivo customers in Q3 FY2026, up nearly 70% year on year ([UC Today, third-party](https://www.uctoday.com/unified-communications/zoom-q3-earnings-workvivo-hits-1225-customers-as-ai-and-virtual-agents-drive-growth/)), and 168 customers above $100,000 ARR in Q2 FY2026, up 142% ([Nasdaq/Zacks, third-party](https://www.nasdaq.com/articles/zooms-enterprise-base-benefits-workvivo-adoption-whats-ahead)).
- Journeys itself launched as an add-on in November 2025, with releases nearly every month since: offboarding trigger and Audience Sync (December 2025), reminders (January 2026), translations, timestamped analytics export and event steps (April 2026), editing of live journeys (May 2026), AI journey generation (July 2026) and removal of the step limit (August 2026) ([What's New](https://www.workvivo.com/new-in-product/)).
- Workvivo is itself a Common Room customer, listed with "40% more meetings booked" ([Common Room customer metrics](https://www.commonroom.io/resources/customers/metrics/), search extract).

**Vendor claims**
- The Journeys page banner claims Leader status in the 2026 Gartner Magic Quadrant for Intranet Packaged Solutions ([Journeys page](https://www.workvivo.com/journeys/)).
- No usage or outcome figures for Journeys specifically are published.

Inference: Workvivo's growth comes from Zoom distribution, the Meta Workplace shutdown and analyst recognition. Journeys is an expansion lever on that base rather than a growth driver in its own right.

#### 2. How they promise results
Qualitative only: new hires get "consistent, personalized support from day one", employees "get productive faster" and HR saves time ([Journeys page](https://www.workvivo.com/journeys/)). Journeys is an add-on enabled through the account team; no list price ([What's New](https://www.workvivo.com/new-in-product/)). Third parties report Workvivo platform deals starting around $20,000 a year, and one buyer cut a $76,000 quote to about $37,000 (third-party, [Capterra](https://www.capterra.com/p/201794/Workvivo/pricing/); [Vendr](https://www.vendr.com/marketplace/workvivo)). Not outcome-priced.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Data in.** HR provisioning file fields (hire date in YYYYMMDD format, departure date, group, department, location) plus in-app events: first login, birthday, anniversary with cadence (for example a fourth anniversary), badge received, event RSVP, joining a Space ([triggers and steps](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31350002642077-Journeys-Triggers-and-Steps)). HR connectors exist for Workday, UKG and BambooHR, and provisioning runs via Entra ID and SCIM ([HR integrations](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/7981384048541-HR-Integrations), search extract).
2. **Enrolment.** A trigger enrols a user if they match the journey's audience. "Self Enrol" creates evergreen journeys people opt into (parental leave, return from absence). Audience Sync pulls newly eligible people into a running journey ([What's New](https://www.workvivo.com/new-in-product/)).
3. **Sequencing.** Steps run in order, top to bottom. "Add an Interval" delays later steps by 1 to 365 days, counted from the trigger date; two intervals cannot be consecutive and each must be later than the last ([triggers and steps](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31350002642077-Journeys-Triggers-and-Steps)).
4. **Typed steps.** Share a message, page, article, update, link, event, org chart or values; assign a survey or badge; enrol into or out of a Space. One content item per step. Messages live in a central Message Hub and can be edited while a journey is live; completed or acknowledged steps keep their status, in-progress ones get the new content ([triggers and steps](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31350002642077-Journeys-Triggers-and-Steps)).
5. **AI drafting.** "AI Journey Creator" takes a free-text prompt plus guided chips (step count, purpose, pacing) and returns title, description, trigger, steps and intervals; the admin can resubmit or insert into the builder. A separate message generator drafts step copy with tone controls and an optional "Include Values" toggle, with Retry, Refine (shorten, lengthen, change tone) and Insert actions ([Journeys AI](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/38630039649565-Journeys-AI)).
6. **Human approval.** Nothing is published until an admin inserts and publishes. AI can be switched on or off per organisation and per editor role ([Journeys AI](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/38630039649565-Journeys-AI)).
7. **Tracking.** Completion, engagement and acknowledgement analytics, exportable with timestamps; manual and automatic reminders before a journey's end date ([What's New](https://www.workvivo.com/new-in-product/)). Journey Admins see all journeys; Journey Managers see only their own.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **State-machine journeys with strict rules.** Sequential steps, interval constraints and "one item per step" keep journeys predictable and analysable. Novel only for intranets; standard in marketing automation.
- **Prompt-to-structure generation.** The LLM outputs a structured object (trigger, steps, intervals), not prose. Model provider: Not published, beyond "powered by Zoom AI" in Zoom's letter (search extract).
- **Acknowledgement steps.** A hard, user-confirmed completion signal, editable on live journeys since May 2026.
- **Live content swap with state preservation.** Content can change mid-flight without resetting people who already completed a step.
- **Per-tenant feature flags.** Triggers and steps are hidden unless enabled by a CSM ([triggers and steps](https://support.workvivo.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31350002642077-Journeys-Triggers-and-Steps)).
- **Stack from jobs:** React, React Native and Laravel (PHP) on AWS ([Zoom careers](https://careers.zoom.us/jobs/software-engineer-php-and-react-workvivo-ireland-remote-ireland), search extract). No AI-specific Workvivo roles found.
- Evaluation or learning loops: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
The trigger data already exists in HR systems, so every journey is personalised from day one with no enrichment. The delivery channel (the employee app) is captive. Templates for common use cases remove the blank page, and AI generation removes it further. Fast monthly releases show the module is being invested in. Zoom provides distribution into a large installed base.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Model each NBC as a "journey" object: trigger (new Tier A account, job change, funding event, tender published), ordered typed steps, intervals counted from the trigger date, and an audience rule. Store it as structured data so it can be reviewed, edited and reported on.
- **Content creation:** Add a "Campaign Creator" that, from one prompt plus chips (number of touches, objective, pacing), returns a full campaign skeleton for review, then a per-step message drafter with Retry, Refine and Insert actions and an "include our value propositions" toggle drawn from the content library.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** Enforce one content asset per step and keep assets in a central hub, so a whitepaper or case study is mapped explicitly to step 3 and can be swapped across all live campaigns without resetting progress.
- **Segmentation:** Let micro-segment membership act as the audience rule, and add an "Audience Sync" so accounts that newly qualify for a segment join the running campaign automatically.
- **Campaign execution:** Offer three modes: evergreen (always on for any account entering a segment), time-bound (trade show, quarter push) and rep-started (a rep enrols an account manually, like Self Enrol). Use long intervals (30 to 90 days) natively, which suits the pilot client's long industrial cycles.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Because outreach runs in Dripify and email outside the tool, add an "acknowledgement" step type where the rep confirms a step was done and records the outcome. Show per-campaign funnels (who is on which step, who stalled) and send reminders before a campaign window ends.
- **Lead scoring:** Use anniversary-style cadence triggers for re-engagement, for example "12 months after closed lost" or "equipment install date plus 5 years".

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
