### 12. Conversica (tagline: Auto-Pilot AI Agents: Engage Every Lead)
**Website:** [conversica.com](https://www.conversica.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** founded as AVA.ai, renamed Conversica in 2014; San Mateo, California, remote-first ([GlobeNewswire 2014](https://www.globenewswire.com/de/news-release/2014/09/18/666984/31614/en/AVA-ai-Announces-Name-Change-to-Conversica.html)) | **Category:** Conversational AI agents that engage and qualify leads over email, SMS and chat; since July 2026 also an automotive "Revenue Activation Engine" (Ignite)

**Identification:** No page with the exact phrase "Auto-Pilot AI Agents: Engage Every Lead" was found after further exact-phrase searches aimed at LinkedIn, Product Hunt, G2 and YouTube. Evidence for Conversica: its own heading "Engage Every Lead, Everywhere" over "Conversica's AI Agents instantly and persistently connect across email, SMS, and chat" ([Conversica webinar page](https://www.conversica.com/?p=279886)), and a core product of autonomous agents that follow up every inbound lead. Alternatives checked: Regie.ai sold "Auto-Pilot Agents" (hyphenated exactly as in the tagline) with a page titled "Auto-Pilot Agents:", but positioned for outbound prospecting, and the page now redirects to RegieGO ([regie.ai/auto-pilot](https://regie.ai/auto-pilot); [Regie Q1 2024 update](https://regie.ai/blog/whats-new-with-regie-ai-q1-2024), search extract). Rox launched "Rox Autopilot" on 31 March 2026 with an "AI XDR" for inbound and outbound ([Rox release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)), but no "every lead" wording was found. Salesforce Agentforce uses "engage every lead" without "auto-pilot" ([Salesforce](https://www.salesforce.com/agentforce/use-cases/sales/)); Famulor sells a voice "Revenue Autopilot" ([Famulor](https://www.famulor.io/product/revenue-autopilot)). **Confidence: Medium.** If the tagline came from a Regie.ai asset, this section should be replaced.

**Differentiator:** Conversica is the longest-running "persistent follow-up agent": it does not find leads, it works every lead a customer already has, over many weeks, and converts replies into a small set of structured lead statuses that tell a rep exactly what to do next. A decade of conversation data, brand-controlled answers grounded in client content, and a status taxonomy wired into CRM are what separate it from newer AI SDRs. In 2026 it narrowed its bet toward automotive dealers with Ignite.

**Source coverage:**
Website: search extract only ([webinar page](https://www.conversica.com/?p=279886); [demo page](https://www.conversica.com/?p=279814)) | Pricing: not published; search extract only, third-party ([ZoomInfo](https://pipeline.zoominfo.com/sales/conversica-pricing)) | Docs: read ([Identifying and Prioritizing Lead Statuses](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/37796810395291-Identifying-and-Prioritizing-Lead-Statuses)); search extract ([Lead Status Definitions](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/360048704392-Lead-Status-Definitions)) | Changelog: search extract only ([help centre "New & Now"](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/community/posts/29941472063131)) | Blog: read in pass 1 ([Conversation Company](https://www.conversica.com/blog/introducing-the-conversation-company)) | Case studies: search extract only, third-party ([casestudies.com](https://www.casestudies.com/vendor/conversica)) | Videos and webinars: search extract only ([webinar Q&A](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/community/posts/30485578902427--Discussion-Webinar-Q-A-All-Treats-No-Tricks-Our-Most-Powerfully-Human-AI-Yet)) | Leadership posts: not found | Funding and press: search extract only ([Business Wire, July 2026](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260726976371/en/)) | Jobs: search extract only, 2 postings ([Staff AI Software Engineer, closed](https://www.choppingblock.ai/jobs/staff-ai-software-engineer-at-conversica)) | Integrations: search extract only ([HubSpot](https://community.hubspot.com/t5/Releases-and-Updates/HubSpot-Connect-Conversica-Integration/ba-p/417796)) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- **July 2026: majority recapitalisation led by a fund managed by Morgan Stanley Expansion Capital**, announced alongside the launch of **Ignite**, "an AI-native Revenue Activation Engine for dealers and dealer groups". Management retains "a significant ownership position"; terms not disclosed ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260726976371/en/), search extract; [Auto Remarketing, third-party](https://www.autoremarketing.com/ar/technology/morgan-stanley-expansion-capital-leads-recapitalization-of-conversica-acquires-majority-stake/)).
- Earlier capital: $25M from Morgan Stanley Expansion Capital (2022) after venture rounds ([Morgan Stanley](https://www.morganstanley.com/im/en-sg/institutional-investor/insights/press-release/conversica-announces-financing-ms-expansion-capital.html)).
- Leader in The Forrester Wave: Conversation Automation Solutions for B2B, Q1 2024 ([Conversica blog](https://www.conversica.com/blog/why-conversica-is-the-choice-for-responsible-enterprise-brands)).
- Automotive dealership agents launched January 2025 ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250121606219/en/Conversica-Announces-Groundbreaking-Car-Buying-Experience-with-New-AI-Agents-For-Automotive-Dealerships)). Open roles in 2026 include a Senior Strategic Account Executive, Automotive ([AI Chopping Block](https://www.choppingblock.ai/jobs/staff-ai-software-engineer-at-conversica)).
- The help centre is active: new articles in 2026 include a Salesforce Flow guide to create an opportunity when a lead "goes Hot", a guide to add contacts to a re-engagement campaign on a schedule, supported conversation languages, and an "Ignite Query Library" ([help centre](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/37796810395291-Identifying-and-Prioritizing-Lead-Statuses)).

**Vendor claims**
- "More than 2,000 successful partnerships" ([Conversica blog](https://www.conversica.com/blog/introducing-the-conversation-company)); "average 10-fold increase in pipeline" and "24x ROI" (vendor claims via [casestudies.com, third-party](https://www.casestudies.com/vendor/conversica)).
- Epson America: 240% more engagement, 75% more MQLs, $2M incremental revenue in 90 days ([BestPractice.ai, third-party](https://www.bestpractice.ai/ai-case-study-best-practice/epson_america_increases_engagement_and_marketing_qualified_leads_by_240%25_and_75%25_respectively_with_ai-powered_sales_assistant)).

Inference: a private equity majority stake plus an automotive-only new product suggests B2B lead engagement is now a mature, slower line and automotive is the growth bet.

#### 2. How they promise results
The promise is coverage and persistence: no lead goes unworked. Agents start "within minutes" of a lead entering CRM and continue until a meeting, a hand-off or an opt-out ([data sheet](https://www.conversica.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/AI-Assistant-for-Conversational-Sales-ds-050221.pdf)). Proof is expressed as engagement lift, MQL lift, influenced pipeline and ROI multiples. Pricing is quote-based and not published; third parties cite about $2,999 per month as a starting point ([ZoomInfo, third-party](https://pipeline.zoominfo.com/sales/conversica-pricing)). Not outcome-priced.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Data in.** Leads and contacts from CRM and marketing automation (Salesforce, HubSpot, Dynamics, Marketo, Eloqua), "50+ out-of-the-box integrations" ([SFMC datasheet](https://www.conversica.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/salesforce-marketing-cloud-datasheet.pdf)). Enrolment is usually driven by the customer's own automation, for example a Salesforce record-triggered flow that adds leads to a Conversica campaign by lead source ([help centre](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/37796810395291-Identifying-and-Prioritizing-Lead-Statuses)).
2. **Conversation.** The agent sends a multi-touch series over email, SMS or chat. Chat, email and SMS are supported; voice had been explored but not added as of the late 2024 webinar ([webinar Q&A](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/community/posts/30485578902427--Discussion-Webinar-Q-A-All-Treats-No-Tricks-Our-Most-Powerfully-Human-AI-Yet), search extract).
3. **Answering questions.** AnswersIQ grounds replies in client-approved content using retrieval ([Salestechstar press release](https://salestechstar.com/price-optimization-revenue-management/conversica-transforms-ai-agents-with-out-of-the-box-brand-controlled-conversations-for-enterprise-revenue-teams/)).
4. **Classification into six lead statuses** ([lead statuses](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/37796810395291-Identifying-and-Prioritizing-Lead-Statuses)):
   - *Hot Lead*: expressed interest ("Yes, let's set up a call"). The agent later checks back that the rep connected.
   - *Further Action* (Lead at Risk): was hot, but says they still have questions or were not contacted.
   - *Lead to Review*: question or request the agent cannot answer, auto-reply, or wrong person. Messaging stops.
   - *Satisfied*, *Disqualified* ("bought elsewhere"), *No Further Action*.
5. **Prioritisation and alerts.** The rep dashboard orders queues as Hot, Further Action, Lead to Review, then starred. Alerts go by email, SMS and a Daily Digest of the prior day's Hot, At Risk and To Review leads ([lead statuses](https://help.conversica.com/hc/en-us/articles/37796810395291-Identifying-and-Prioritizing-Lead-Statuses)).
6. **Write-back and automation.** Statuses sync to CRM fields; documented flows create a Salesforce opportunity when a lead goes Hot. The HubSpot integration logs every drip email and text ([HubSpot community](https://community.hubspot.com/t5/Releases-and-Updates/HubSpot-Connect-Conversica-Integration/ba-p/417796), search extract).
7. **Human-in-the-loop.** Autonomous until intent is detected; humans take over at Hot or Lead to Review. Reps can also "reply as assistant" or "skip to follow up" and "stop messaging".

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Multi-model generation** using models from OpenAI, Meta and Google (October 2024 release) ([CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/the-wire/conversica-transforms-ai-agents-with-out-of-the-box-brand-controlled-conversations-for-enterprise-revenue-team/)).
- **Client-specific models** trained automatically on brand assets plus a curated general dataset ([Conversica](https://www.conversica.com/?p=276330)); claimed training on over a billion conversations (vendor claim via [YesPress, third-party](https://yespress.io/conversica)).
- **RAG with guardrails** (AnswersIQ) to reduce hallucination; the February 2025 demo shows "managing data and adding guardrails" ([demo page](https://www.conversica.com/?p=279814), search extract).
- **Intent classification to an action taxonomy.** The novel, durable asset is the mapping from free-text reply to a small set of operational statuses, each with a default rep action and alert. Classifier design and accuracy: Not published.
- **Ignite context layer:** connects customer, vehicle, sales, service and conversation data across dealer systems "without requiring a CRM replacement" to flag events such as lease or equity milestones ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260726976371/en/), search extract).
- **Stack from jobs:** Python and production LLM experience for a Staff AI Software Engineer (posted January 2026, closed 31 July 2026); a Senior Backend Platform Engineer on Lever ([AI Chopping Block](https://www.choppingblock.ai/jobs/staff-ai-software-engineer-at-conversica); [Employbl](https://www.employbl.com/jobs/senior-backend-platform-engineer-Conversica-1561796), search extract).
- Deliverability infrastructure and evaluation loops: Not published. A third party reports no public API ([Dashly, third-party](https://www.dashly.io/tools/ai-sdr-tools/conversica/)).

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
Most inbound and aged leads are never followed up properly; a patient agent that keeps asking, then hands over at the moment of intent, recovers pipeline that already exists. The status taxonomy makes the output usable by busy reps. Enterprise guardrails earn trust with brand-sensitive buyers. Weaknesses: it depends on the customer's lead volume, integration is managed by Conversica rather than self-serve, and reporting outside Salesforce is reported as weak ([Dashly, third-party](https://www.dashly.io/tools/ai-sdr-tools/conversica/)).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Adopt a six-state reply taxonomy (Hot, At Risk, To Review, Satisfied, Disqualified, No Further Action) with a default rep action for each. Let reps paste or import replies from Dripify and email so the tool classifies them, even before any sending is automated.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Order the rep's daily queue by status first (Hot, then At Risk, then To Review) and by score second, and send a daily digest in that order.
- **Deal:** After a lead is handed to a rep, schedule an automatic check-back ("did someone contact you?") and flag "At Risk" if not; long industrial cycles lose deals in exactly this gap.
- **Lead scoring:** Feed the reply status back into the score so a "Disqualified: bought elsewhere" decays the account and a "Not now" sets a recontact date.
- **Content creation:** Ground answers and drafts in the approved content library (retrieval), and stop and route to a human whenever the draft would need information not in the library.
- **Campaign execution:** Add a re-engagement campaign that enrols aged or closed-lost accounts on a schedule, mirroring Conversica's schedule-triggered flow.
- **Prospecting:** Note the strategic lesson: Conversica moved toward a vertical (automotive) with a context layer across the systems dealers already use. Demand AI's equivalent is industrial-specific signals and terminology.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 13. Common Room
**Website:** [commonroom.io](https://www.commonroom.io/) | **Founded / HQ:** about 2020, Seattle; founders Linda Lian, Viraj Mody, Tom Kleinpeter, Francis Luu ([GeekWire, third-party](https://www.geekwire.com/2021/seattle-startup-common-room-emerges-stealth-mode-52m-funding/)) | **Category:** AI-native GTM intelligence: signal capture, person-level identity resolution, scoring, buying committee mapping and AI research agents (RoomieAI)

**Differentiator:** Common Room resolves signals from 50+ sources (web, product, community, social, CRM, intent) to a named person and account, then keeps everything downstream transparent: its new AI scoring uses an LLM to *write* a rules-based score from the customer's own closed-won data, which a human reviews and activates, rather than a black-box model. Its new Buying Committee view applies one LLM-derived "who buys what we sell" model to every account and shows engagement status per role. It also exposes all of this to customers' own AI tools through an MCP server and Claude connector.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read in pass 1 ([homepage](https://www.commonroom.io/)) | Pricing: search extract only, third-party ([Salesmotion](https://salesmotion.io/blog/common-room-pricing); [Valley](https://www.joinvalley.co/blog/common-room-pricing-explained-2026)) | Docs: read, 3 pages ([core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/); [AI-Native Scoring](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/ai-native-scoring/); [Buying Committee](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/buying-committee/)) | Changelog: search extract only ([Q1 2026 release webinar](https://goldcast.ondemand.goldcast.io/on-demand/2de8d442-8af8-403b-8c99-0b6a3e5bd585)) | Blog: read in pass 1 ([Person360 launch](https://www.commonroom.io/blog/introducing-person360-connect-with-the-person-behind-the-signal/)) | Case studies: search extract only ([customer metrics](https://www.commonroom.io/resources/customers/metrics/)) | Videos and webinars: search extract only (Goldcast) | Leadership posts: not found | Funding and press: read ([Zoom investor release](https://investors.zoom.us/news-releases/news-release-details/zoom-acquire-common-room-bringing-buyer-intelligence-its-ai)) | Jobs: search extract only, 1 posting ([Data Engineer](https://www.builtinla.com/job/data-engineer/9057518)) | Integrations: read (docs integration index on [core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/)) | Investor filings: not applicable (private; acquirer Zoom's release read)

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- **Zoom signed a definitive agreement to acquire Common Room on 2 July 2026**, to bring its signals and RoomieAI agents into Zoom Revenue Accelerator; terms not disclosed, "expected to close in the coming weeks" ([Zoom investor release](https://investors.zoom.us/news-releases/news-release-details/zoom-acquire-common-room-bringing-buyer-intelligence-its-ai)). As of 2 August 2026 the deal was listed as "close pending" ([Cleanlist, third-party](https://www.cleanlist.ai/blog/2026-08-02-b2b-data-tool-acquisitions-and-shutdowns-2026)); no completion notice was found.
- Funding: $52M at stealth exit in 2021 (Greylock, Index, Madrona) ([GeekWire, third-party](https://www.geekwire.com/2021/seattle-startup-common-room-emerges-stealth-mode-52m-funding/)), about $105M total reported (third-party, [Welcome to the Jungle](https://app.welcometothejungle.com/jobs/EnGeeV1A/company)).
- The product is still shipping fast: AI-Native Scoring (beta) and Buying Committee (beta) docs were created in September 2026 ([AI-Native Scoring](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/ai-native-scoring/); [Buying Committee](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/buying-committee/)). The docs index also lists a DataAgent, a CLI, a Claude Connector and Plugin, an MCP server, signal trend tracking, and native Zoom Engage and Zoom Dialer integrations ([core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/)).
- Named customers: Semgrep, Notion, Temporal, Superhuman, Workvivo ([customer metrics](https://www.commonroom.io/resources/customers/metrics/), search extract).

**Vendor claims**
- Semgrep 74% pipeline growth in a quarter; Notion 30% more meetings per rep per month; Workvivo 40% more meetings booked ([customer metrics](https://www.commonroom.io/resources/customers/metrics/), search extract); Temporal over 50% of meetings via Common Room ([casestudies.com, third-party](https://www.casestudies.com/company/common-room)).

#### 2. How they promise results
"Pipeline has a new equation": find, time and convert using signals ([homepage](https://www.commonroom.io/)). Outcomes are pipeline growth, meetings per rep and share of meetings sourced. Pricing is annual and seat, contact and credit based, not outcome based. Third parties report an Essential tier between about $1,700 and $2,500 per month billed annually, with 5 seats, up to 100,000 tracked contacts and monthly RoomieAI and Prospector credits; Advanced and Enterprise by quote; onboarding $5,000 to $20,000; typical contracts about $27,000 to $30,000 a year (third-party, [Valley](https://www.joinvalley.co/blog/common-room-pricing-explained-2026); [Salesmotion](https://salesmotion.io/blog/common-room-pricing); [Warmly, competitor](https://www.warmly.ai/p/blog/common-room-pricing)). Official price list: Not confirmed.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Connect signals.** Native and integrated sources: website tracking (known and anonymous, "Enhanced US Tracking"), job changes, job listings, news, technographics; CRM (Salesforce managed package, HubSpot); sequencers (Outreach, Salesloft, Smartlead, Groove); intent (Bombora, 6sense, Demandbase, TechTarget, G2); call tools (Gong, Zoom); warehouses (Snowflake, BigQuery, Redshift, S3); community and social (Slack, Discord, GitHub, Reddit, LinkedIn, YouTube, Stack Overflow); custom via API, Zapier or Google Sheets ([core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/)).
2. **Resolve identity.** Person360 merges each person's signals across sources into one Contact and enriches it through a waterfall across "dozens of providers" ([core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/); [Person360](https://commonroom.io/product/person-360), search extract).
3. **Score.** AI-Native Scoring reads the workspace's closed-won deals, roles and seniorities on those deals, firmographics, technographics, intent, web activity and custom fields, then generates criteria, point values (-10 to +10) and a Fit and Behaviour balance with a plain-language summary. The admin previews distribution, matches per band and specific known-good or known-bad records, then activates. Output is a percentile or a fixed point value ([AI-Native Scoring](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/ai-native-scoring/)).
4. **Map the buying committee.** An LLM researches the customer's own company to infer which departments, seniorities and titles buy its product; that model is applied to every account. Each cell shows the best contacts (existing or from the 200M prospect dataset) with status Engaged, Warm or Not engaged over a 30-day lookback ([Buying Committee](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/buying-committee/)).
5. **Segment and act.** Segments drive tasks, statuses, Salesforce sync and sequence enrolment; workflows automate with custom logic; team alerts post to Slack ([core concepts](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/get-started/core-concepts/)).
6. **Generate.** RoomieAI researches organisations and drafts messages; Spark Brief emails a daily digest ([Spark Brief](https://commonroom.io/product/ai/sparkbrief/)).
7. **Human approval.** Scores are saved disabled until activated; drafts are reviewed by reps.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **LLM as model author, not model.** "AI-Native Scoring uses a large language model to recommend a transparent, rules-based score"; it does not learn on its own, criteria that cannot be mapped to data are shown as unavailable "rather than invented", and nothing scores until activated ([AI-Native Scoring](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/using-common-room/ai-native-scoring/)). This is the most transferable idea in this batch for Demand AI.
- **Score QA tooling.** Distribution view to catch "everyone is 99th percentile", per-criterion applicability counts and record spot checks before go-live.
- **Scores carry their reasons downstream.** Scores sync to Salesforce and HubSpot "including the key factors behind each record's score".
- **Quality bar over confidence scores.** Buying Committee hides low-confidence suggestions instead of showing a probability.
- **Agent-ready data layer.** MCP server at a single endpoint with OAuth, usable from Claude and other assistants; scores and committee data are available through it ([MCP docs](https://www.commonroom.io/docs/signals/mcp), search extract).
- **Data infrastructure from jobs:** ETL pipelines processing "billions of events daily" on AWS, Postgres and Node.js ([Data Engineer posting](https://www.builtinla.com/job/data-engineer/9057518), search extract).
- Matching algorithm accuracy and models used: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
A wedge in signals that older intent vendors missed (community, developer, product usage), then broadening to every first-party signal. Person-level resolution turns "account surging" into "this person did this", which reps act on. Transparency (explainable scores, visible reasons, human activation) builds rep trust. Exposing data to customers' own agents via MCP keeps it central as buyers adopt general AI assistants. Zoom's purchase shows signal layers are valued as inputs to conversation platforms.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** Build "AI-drafted, human-activated" scoring: an LLM reads the client's won-deal list and ICP notes and proposes fit and behaviour rules with weights and a plain-English rationale; the user edits, previews, then activates. Keep Demand AI's rules engine as the runtime. Mark any rule that cannot be mapped to uploaded columns as "unavailable", never invented.
- **Lead scoring:** Add a score preview panel: distribution across tiers, how many accounts each rule touches, and spot checks on known-good and known-bad accounts before tiers go live.
- **Qualified lead identification:** Add a buying committee grid per account (department by seniority) built from one product-level model of who buys industrial equipment (plant manager, maintenance head, procurement, engineering), showing Engaged, Warm or Not engaged from logged activity.
- **Segmentation:** Let segments carry actions (task, status, export to Dripify list) so a segment is a unit of work, not just a filter.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** When the committee shows a single engaged contact, recommend a "multi-thread" action targeting the empty critical cells.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Export scores with their top three reasons into CSV and CRM so reps see why outside the tool.
- **Prospecting:** Plan an MCP or API endpoint later so clients' own AI assistants can query Demand AI scores and recommendations.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach

### 14. Warmly
**Website:** [warmly.ai](https://www.warmly.ai/) | **Founded / HQ:** Y Combinator company, San Francisco ([YC profile](https://www.ycombinator.com/companies/warmly)); co-founders Max Greenwald (CEO) and Alan Zhao ([Revenue Rebels](https://www.warmly.ai/p/media/revenue-rebels-season-3), search extract) | **Category:** Person-level website visitor identification, intent signals and AI GTM agents (inbound, outbound, data); being acquired by HubSpot

**Differentiator:** Warmly starts from the most visible pain (anonymous website visitors), identifies them to person level, and then runs agents that act in real time on that visit: AI chat, routing, sequencing other personas at the same account, and ad retargeting. Its "Context Graph" records not just signals but the actions taken, the reasoning behind them and the outcomes, so every later decision, human or AI, can see why something was done and whether it worked.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)) | Pricing: read, now quote only ([pricing page](https://www.warmly.ai/p/pricing)); third-party search extract ([Abmatic](https://blog.abmatic.ai/warmly-pricing-guide-2026)) | Docs: read 1 article ([Key Plays](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/8166109272-warmly-key-plays-chat-orchestrator)); search extract ([REST API](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/9641856032-warmly-technical-documentation-rest-api); [AI Chat Prompts](https://help.warmly.ai/en/articles/8286673-ai-chat-prompts)) | Changelog: not found | Blog: search extract only ([HubSpot announcement](https://www.warmly.ai/p/blog/warmly-is-joining-hubspot)) | Case studies: search extract only ([customer story](https://warmly.ai/p/customers/behavioral-signals)) | Videos and podcasts: search extract only ([GTM Engineer podcast](https://thegtmengineer.substack.com/p/how-warmly-hit-5m-arr-after-5-pivots)) | Leadership posts: search extract only (CEO blog) | Funding and press: search extract only ([CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-acquires-warmly-to-boost-ai-sales-agents/)) | Jobs: read, 1 posting ([Staff AI Engineer](https://www.builtinsf.com/job/technical-staff-ai-engineer/9913183)) | Integrations: read (listed on [Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph) and in the job posting) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- **HubSpot agreed to acquire Warmly, announced 30 June 2026**; terms not disclosed; existing contracts, pricing and integrations unchanged for now ([Warmly CEO blog](https://www.warmly.ai/p/blog/warmly-is-joining-hubspot); [CMSWire, third-party](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-acquires-warmly-to-boost-ai-sales-agents/)). Completion on 1 July 2026 is reported by one third party only ([Opensend, third-party](https://www.opensend.com/post/warmly-review)). The site now carries a "HubSpot is acquiring Warmly" banner ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)).
- HubSpot was Warmly's first integration (2023) and grew from 20 connected customers to 223 paying customers ([CMSWire, third-party](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-acquires-warmly-to-boost-ai-sales-agents/)).
- Funding: $11M Series A (October 2023) and $6M Series A+ led by RTP Global (February 2025); investors include Felicis and NFX ([Warmly blog](https://warmly.ai/p/blog/warmly-closes-17-million-in-series-a); [IntelligentCIO, third-party](https://www.intelligentcio.com/north-america/2025/02/05/warmly-closes-6-million-in-series-a-funding-round-led-by-rtp-global/)).
- Traction: a Warmly job posting (removed 11 October 2026) says the company is "scaling from $3M to $10M in ARR and 250 to 500 customers in advance of a Series B" and that "top champions are already buying for Inbound Identity" ([Staff AI Engineer posting](https://www.builtinsf.com/job/technical-staff-ai-engineer/9913183)). A podcast cites $5M ARR after five pivots ([GTM Engineer, third-party](https://thegtmengineer.substack.com/p/how-warmly-hit-5m-arr-after-5-pivots), search extract).

**Vendor claims**
- WorkBoard: pipeline targets up 20% without headcount; Tovuti: average contract value doubled; Qase: Warmly-sourced MQLs closed 50% higher and 30% faster ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)).
- Kargo "4x'd qualified pipeline in a month" from an ads strategy; StraightIn $4 cost per lead on a whitepaper to Warmly-identified contacts ([Key Plays](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/8166109272-warmly-key-plays-chat-orchestrator)).
- Warmly also published an anti-case study about a churned customer ([casestudies.com, third-party](https://www.casestudies.com/company/warmly)).

#### 2. How they promise results
"97% of your website visitors leave without filling out a form"; Warmly names them and engages them automatically ([visitor identification](https://warmly.ai/p/solutions/use-cases/website-visitor-identification)). The official pricing page now shows no prices: "We'll customize a plan" ([pricing page](https://www.warmly.ai/p/pricing)). Third parties report an entry tier around $10,000 a year for de-anonymisation, with TAM and inbound agent tiers from about $15,000 to $30,000 a year, and disagree on whether a free tier still exists (third-party, [Abmatic](https://blog.abmatic.ai/warmly-pricing-guide-2026); [Opensend](https://www.opensend.com/post/warmly-review)). The REST API gives 250 free credits a month with paid tiers from $99 ([REST API doc](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/9641856032-warmly-technical-documentation-rest-api), search extract). Packaged by agent and job, not by outcome.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Signals in.** Install the website pixel; connect CRM (HubSpot or Salesforce, bidirectional), sequencer (Outreach or Salesloft), Bombora intent, social signals and job data ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)). Signal types: pricing and demo page visits, return visits, Bombora topic surges, G2 research, competitor comparisons, LinkedIn engagement, funding, leadership changes, job posts, email engagement.
2. **Identify.** Company and person-level de-anonymisation using a proprietary "Inbound Identity graph" ([job posting](https://www.builtinsf.com/job/technical-staff-ai-engineer/9913183)). Warmly's own article puts person-level coverage at about 15% of traffic ([Warmly article](https://www.warmly.ai/p/article/gtm-agent-harness-with-person-level-id-warmly-vs-alternatives)).
3. **Unify.** Everything lands in the Context Graph as four linked types: what happened (signals), what you did (actions), the notes around it (reasoning, for example "Reached out because CFO visited pricing 3x this week"), and what resulted (meetings, stage changes, closed won or lost). All signals "feed one score" ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)).
4. **Act through plays.** Chat plays: basic inbound, closed-lost resurfacing, active deal on site, pricing page workflow, ads visitors. Orchestrator plays: sequence ICP personas at visiting accounts, closed-lost resurfacing, competitor research page triggers ([Key Plays](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/8166109272-warmly-key-plays-chat-orchestrator)).
5. **AI chat.** AI-generated or static prompts, with timed delays (about 8 seconds before the first message, about 12 more before the second) and a fallback if the model call fails ([AI Chat Prompts](https://help.warmly.ai/en/articles/8286673-ai-chat-prompts), search extract).
6. **Learn.** An "OODA+L" loop: observe, orient, decide, act, then outcomes feed back ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)).
7. **Human-in-the-loop.** Reps receive routed leads and Slack alerts and can take over chats.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Decision traces as data.** The Context Graph stores the reason for each action alongside the action and outcome, enabling attribution (ad, visit, meeting, close), Closed Lost resurfacing "when conditions change" and "AI governance: audit why any AI decision was made" ([Context Graph](https://www.warmly.ai/p/context-graph)). How links are formed and stored: Not published.
- **Stack from jobs:** TypeScript, Node.js, GraphQL, tRPC, React; PostgreSQL, Redis, Elasticsearch on Google Cloud with Kubernetes and Docker; integrations with Salesforce, HubSpot, Pipedrive, Outreach, Apollo, Slack; AI work in Python with LangChain or LlamaIndex, vector databases and OpenAI or Anthropic APIs. The role aims to move "from prompt engineering to agentic reasoning" that is "fast enough for real-time web traffic", and to build pipelines that ingest "millions of signals" to score "Strategic/Executive Value" ([job posting](https://www.builtinsf.com/job/technical-staff-ai-engineer/9913183)).
- **API and MCP.** REST API at a per-org key with four tool endpoints (updated June 2026) ([REST API doc](https://help.warmly.ai/articles/9641856032-warmly-technical-documentation-rest-api), search extract), plus a hosted MCP server ([apis.io, third-party](https://apis.io/providers/warmly/)).
- Identity graph construction and match methodology: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
A visible, immediate "aha" (named visitors on day one), a low-friction script install and plays packaged as templates. Acting within seconds of a visit beats batch scoring. The Context Graph positions them for attribution and learning, which matters as agents take more actions. Weaknesses: person-level match rates of roughly 10 to 15% ([Genesys Growth, third-party](https://genesysgrowth.com/tools/warmly-ai)), publicly acknowledged churn, and now dependence on HubSpot's roadmap; Futurum expects the capability to favour HubSpot-native customers ([Futurum, third-party](https://futurumgroup.com/insights/hubspots-warmly-acquisition-embeds-real-time-buyer-intent-directly-into-the-crm-layer/)).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Build a per-account timeline with four record types: signal, action, reason, outcome. Every NBA the tool recommends and the rep accepts is logged with its reason ("recommended because new plant expansion announced") and later linked to its outcome.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Ship a library of named plays, as Warmly does: closed-lost resurfacing, competitor research, pricing or product page visit, active deal re-engagement. Each play has a trigger, a target persona set and a step template.
- **Lead scoring:** Show signal provenance by class (first-party web, third-party intent, company change) in the score breakdown, and allow "high intent page" rules (for example a product specification or brochure download page) to override decay.
- **Qualified lead identification:** Even without a pixel, accept a website visit export (company-level) as a signal file; company-level matching is enough for account-based industrial selling.
- **Campaign execution:** When one person at an account shows intent, recommend sequencing the other ICP personas at that account (Warmly's orchestrator play), mapped to Demand AI's micro-segments.
- **Deal:** Use the outcome links to produce simple attribution ("signals present in won deals") that feeds ICP refinement.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 15. Rox
**Website:** [rox.com](https://www.rox.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** founded 2024, emerged November 2024; San Francisco ([Bloomberg, third-party](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2024-11-19/sequoia-gv-back-startup-building-ai-agents-for-salespeople); [Rox pricing footer](https://www.rox.com/pricing)) | **Category:** AI-native "revenue operating system": warehouse-native knowledge graph plus an "Agent Swarm" of revenue agents, now with sequencing, dialer and meeting capture

**Differentiator:** Rox builds the data layer first (a unified knowledge graph over CRM, email, meetings, warehouse and public data) and scopes each agent to one seller and one account pair, so every output is grounded in that account's context. It is unusually open about how it works: public engineering docs describe its RAG insight filtering, its sandbox evaluation suite of about 200 scenarios with LLM-as-judge, and a weekly changelog. Pricing is metered in "Agent Actions", so customers pay for agent work rather than seats.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read ([pricing](https://www.rox.com/pricing)) | Pricing: read ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)) | Docs: read, 5 pages ([llms.txt index](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt); [Pipeline Generation](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/workflow-specific-agents/discover/pipeline-generation.md); [Evaluation Framework](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/evaluation-framework.md); [Agent triggers](https://docs.rox.com/development/user-guide/custom-agents/agent-triggers.md); [architecture](https://docs.rox.com/development/how-rox-works/rox-platform-architecture) in pass 1) | Changelog: read ([weekly release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)) | Engineering blog: read ([RAG Pipeline for Insight Filtering](https://docs.rox.com/development/blogs/rag-pipeline-for-insight-filtering.md)) | Case studies: read, quotes only ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)) | Videos: search extract only ([Bloomberg video](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/videos/2024-11-19/rox-s-revenue-efficiency-goals-video)) | Founder posts: not read (founder notes listed in [llms.txt](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt)) | Funding and press: search extract only, third-party ([Dealroom](https://dealroom.co/news/126775-ai-sales-startup-rox-hits-1-2b-valuation-and-8m-arr-with-new-round/)) | Jobs: read, 36 postings ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/Rox-Data-Corp)) | Integrations: read (docs index in [llms.txt](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt)) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- Launched November 2024 with $50M: Sequoia-led seed (GV participating) and General Catalyst-led Series A ([Bloomberg, third-party](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2024-11-19/sequoia-gv-back-startup-building-ai-agents-for-salespeople)). General availability announced 16 September 2025 ([Rox GA post](https://www.rox.com/articles/rox-is-now-generally-available), search extract).
- Reported (TechCrunch, March 2026, unnamed sources): a General Catalyst-led round at about $1.2B valuation, with about $8M ARR projected for end of 2025; not confirmed by either party ([Dealroom, third-party](https://dealroom.co/news/126775-ai-sales-startup-rox-hits-1-2b-valuation-and-8m-arr-with-new-round/)).
- Gartner's Magic Quadrant for Revenue Action Orchestration (published 13 October 2026) covers 13 vendors including Rox; Rox was not in the December 2025 edition ([Gartner abstract](https://www.gartner.com/en/documents/8500777), search extract). Rox's banner says it is "the Only Visionary" ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)); the placement itself is behind Gartner's paywall and not independently confirmed.
- Hiring: 36 open roles, 13 in GTM (strategic AEs in Austin, Chicago, London, Australia, San Francisco), 8 in engineering (Foundations Engineers for agents, deep infra and deep platform; a Founding Applied Research Engineer; Bengaluru backend), plus forward deployed engineers ([Ashby](https://jobs.ashbyhq.com/Rox-Data-Corp)).
- Named customers with quotes: XBOW, Upwind, MongoDB, Cloud Software Group, Together AI, Pallet ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)).

**Vendor claims**
- Upwind: "we just saved you 80% of your day"; Pallet: "our best quarter yet" ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)). Early customers: 50% higher rep productivity, 2x revenue per rep (AWS co-marketing, [aws-news mirror](https://aws-news.com/article/2025-10-01-rox-accelerates-sales-productivity-with-ai-agents-powered-by-amazon-bedrock)).
- Pipeline generation turns "a 5+ hour weekly process into a 10-minute automated workflow" ([Pipeline Generation](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/workflow-specific-agents/discover/pipeline-generation.md)).

#### 2. How they promise results
"Revenue on Autopilot" (trademark) and "Only pay for agent work". Pricing combines outcome-based and usage-based elements: each task (account research, meeting brief, pipeline generation, insights) costs a defined number of Agent Actions, weighted by model and data used. Individual $100 a month (10,000 actions), Teams $255 a month (15,000 actions, unlimited seats, CRM write-back), Enterprise custom (warehouse sync, SAML, SCIM). Unused actions do not roll over; an activity log shows consumption; always-on Insights can be tuned to save actions ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)). In October 2026 Rox shipped a "Value App" that shows pipeline sourced, meetings booked and an estimate of seller time saved, using customer-adjustable assumptions ([release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)). The footer disclaims guaranteed outcomes.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Data in.** CRM (Salesforce with field mapping for accounts, contacts and leads; HubSpot), Gmail or Outlook, calendar, Gong, Zoom and Teams transcripts, Slack, warehouses (Snowflake, Databricks, BigQuery) by native sharing or replication, plus public data: news, job postings, blogs, financial documents ([llms.txt](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt); [Pipeline Generation](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/workflow-specific-agents/discover/pipeline-generation.md)).
2. **System of Context.** Entity resolution and relationships across accounts, people, opportunities and interactions on a "TB+ scale data lakehouse" ([Pipeline Generation](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/workflow-specific-agents/discover/pipeline-generation.md)).
3. **Enrichment.** A published list of 15 providers queried programmatically (FullEnrich, ContactOut, ZeroBounce, Hunter, RocketReach, People Data Labs, EnrichLayer, CrustData, Prospeo, Datagma, Icypeas, Findymail, Wiza, PredictLeads, Mixrank) with location and purpose ([pricing page](https://www.rox.com/pricing)); customers can add their own provider keys ([llms.txt](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt)).
4. **Prioritise.** Pipe Gen Actions combine "seller-defined prioritization criteria" with agent reasoning to continuously stack-rank accounts, re-evaluating as signals arrive (event-driven, not batch) ([Pipeline Generation](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/workflow-specific-agents/discover/pipeline-generation.md)).
5. **Prospect.** Since September 2026, auto-prospecting: describe the buyer, the agent finds contacts, labels each Good Fit or Bad Fit "with the reasoning next to it", and moves good ones into a sequence ([release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)).
6. **Engage.** Sequences across email, LinkedIn and phone, reply classification (out of office, unsubscribe, interested, not interested; April 2026), auto-reply and meeting scheduling, Dialer 2.0 (October 2026) ([release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)).
7. **Automate.** Custom agents start on a schedule, before or after a meeting, on transcript completion, on opportunity activity, on webhook, or when a "Clever Column" cell is generated ([Agent triggers](https://docs.rox.com/development/user-guide/custom-agents/agent-triggers.md)).
8. **Human-in-the-loop.** Agents are scoped to one seller and account pair, and "critical actions" are sent for human review ([Agent Swarm docs](https://docs.rox.com/development/engineering/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer), search extract); suggested agents must be reviewed before activation.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **RAG insight filtering.** Three Turbopuffer vector indexes (preference keywords, user preferences, public artefacts). Titles and content of each incoming article are embedded and matched separately against preferences, taking the maximum similarity; a dual threshold splits users into "exclusive" (high confidence) and "non-exclusive" audiences ([RAG Pipeline](https://docs.rox.com/development/blogs/rag-pipeline-for-insight-filtering.md)).
- **Evaluation framework.** A sandbox mocks each service behind strict interfaces; about 200 common queries run with heuristic checks or LLM-as-judge; production edge cases become new scenarios ([Evaluation Framework](https://docs.rox.com/development/architecture/docs/agent-swarm-tm-layer/evaluation-framework.md)).
- **"Recursive self-improvement for revenue"** (October 2026): Rox watches recurring chat requests and proposes agent workflows with instructions, a trigger and the source conversations, for user review ([release notes](https://docs.rox.com/development/release-notes/release-notes.md)).
- **Clever Columns:** LLM-generated research fields per account that can themselves trigger agents ([Agent triggers](https://docs.rox.com/development/user-guide/custom-agents/agent-triggers.md)).
- **Models:** Anthropic Claude on Amazon Bedrock per AWS; an OpenAI customer story also exists ([AWS](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/rox-accelerates-sales-productivity-with-ai-agents-powered-by-amazon-bedrock); [OpenAI](https://openai.com/mt-MT/index/rox/)). Inference: multi-provider.
- **Governance:** organisation-wide defaults, pods, permission sets and Access Provenance; API and MCP for enrichment, prospecting and sequences ([llms.txt](https://docs.rox.com/development/llms.txt)).
- The account prioritisation scoring model itself: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
Heavy capital and a founder network into enterprise buyers (CEO Ishan Mukherjee, former New Relic chief growth officer; [CB Insights, third-party](https://www.cbinsights.com/company/roxai)). A context-first architecture makes outputs account-specific. Weekly shipping has expanded Rox from research into a full engagement stack (sequences, dialer, meetings), letting it replace tools. Self-serve at $100 a month plus enterprise sales and forward deployed engineers covers both ends. Inference: the valuation reflects platform ambition more than current revenue.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** Show a "Good Fit / Bad Fit" label with one line of reasoning beside every prospect, as Rox's auto-prospecting does, alongside Demand AI's tier.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Re-rank on events, not on a schedule: when a new signal file is uploaded, recompute only the affected accounts and highlight movers.
- **Prospecting:** For signal matching, embed each signal's headline and body separately and take the higher similarity to the client's interest keywords (for example "plant expansion", "capex", "new line"); use two thresholds to split "send to rep now" from "add to digest".
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Add reply classification (out of office, unsubscribe, interested, not interested) for imported replies.
- **Content creation:** Build a small evaluation set (about 50 realistic accounts and expected outputs) and run every prompt change against it, with simple checks plus an LLM judge; add each production failure as a new case.
- **Campaign execution:** Offer schedule, webhook and "after meeting" triggers for recommendations, and later suggest automations from repeated user requests.
- **Deal:** Ship a "value" view early: accounts worked, meetings, pipeline attributed and estimated hours saved with editable assumptions, which gives the pilot a shared scorecard without inventing figures.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach

### 17. AiSDR
**Website:** [aisdr.com](https://aisdr.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2023, San Francisco, Ukrainian founding team (Yuriy Zaremba, CEO; Oleg Zaremba, CTO) ([AIN.UA interview](https://en.ain.ua/2024/02/13/interview-with-yuriy-zaremba-from-aisdr)) | **Category:** AI SDR / autonomous outbound agent ("Ami, AI GTM engineer")

**Differentiator:** AiSDR sells the whole outbound loop (strategy, list, copy, deliverability, replies, and post-launch repair) as a pay-per-contact utility with no subscription, rather than as seats. Its agent, Ami, is framed as a strategist that pushes back on weak ICPs and asks for "public evidence the pain is live" before it builds a campaign. It also publishes its own A/B experiments, losses included, which is unusual in this category.

**Source coverage:**
Website/product pages: read ([pricing for LLMs](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/), [experiments hub](https://aisdr.com/outreach-experiments/)) | Pricing: read ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)) | Docs/help centre: read, 1 article in full ([Meet Ami](https://help.aisdr.com/articles/6019842681-meet-ami-your-ai-gtm-engineer)) | Changelog: not found (third-party pricing tracker only) | Blog: read (experiment index, 6 posts listed) | Case studies: search extract only (pass 1 sources retained) | Video/YouTube: search extract only (CEO video embedded in [report page](https://aisdr.com/ai-sdr-industry-report/)) | Founder posts/podcasts: read (pass 1 interview retained) | Funding/press: read (pass 1 sources), 2026 round unverified | Jobs: not found (no careers page or ATS listing surfaced) | Integrations/marketplaces: read (list on [pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- About $3M seed in November 2023 from Y Combinator (S23), Flyer One Ventures, SID Venture Partners, Pioneer Fund, Rebel Fund and angels ([AIN.UA](https://en.ain.ua/2023/11/29/ukrainian-ai-startup-aisdr-raises-3m), [The SaaS News](https://www.thesaasnews.com/news/aisdr-raises-3-million-in-funding/)). An April 2026 seed with no disclosed amount appears only on aggregators (third-party, unverified) ([vcbacked](https://www.vcbacked.co/company/aisdr), [FundedIQ](https://fundediq.co/aisdr-aisdr-com-funding/)).
- CEO Yuriy Zaremba previously sold AXDRAFT to Onit ([AIN.UA interview](https://en.ain.ua/2024/02/13/interview-with-yuriy-zaremba-from-aisdr)).
- The pricing page was rewritten on 15 October 2026 to a pure pay-as-you-go model; the plain-text version is dated "Last updated: 2026-10-15" ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)). A third-party tracker logged an earlier change in August 2026 ([CostBench](https://costbench.com/changelog/aisdr-pricing-update-2026-08/), third-party).
- Active content operation: a public experiments tracker with six completed write-ups between July and October 2026 ([experiments hub](https://aisdr.com/outreach-experiments/)).

**Vendor claims**
- "Trusted by 250+ companies"; homepage live stats of $123.3M influenced pipeline and 1,032 meetings in 30 days (pass 1, [homepage](https://aisdr.com/)).
- Ami "trained on 17K+ real campaigns" (site navigation, [pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)); "14,000+ campaigns" in the help centre ([Meet Ami](https://help.aisdr.com/articles/6019842681-meet-ami-your-ai-gtm-engineer)); 18K in pass 1. The figure is inconsistent across first-party pages.
- Case studies: Medisafe "29 meetings in 30 days" and a Fortune 500 client at 6.19% reply rate (pass 1, [Medisafe](https://aisdr.com/case-study-medisafe/)). The Medisafe contact shares the founders' surname; no relationship is stated.

#### 2. How they promise results
- Meeting-rate benchmarks: "1 to 3 qualified meetings per 100 leads" (vendor claim, pass 1, [homepage](https://aisdr.com/)).
- Industry report positioned as proof of category value (vendor research): median 22.7 days to first positive response; meetings per SDR rising from 3.5 to 16.2 per month by month 6 "in successful deployments"; most failed rollouts collapse within 9 weeks; a "70/30" AI-to-human split among teams that scale; "50% of failed AI SDR teams had perfectly personalized copy but lost on context and timing" ([report](https://aisdr.com/ai-sdr-industry-report/)). Method and sample (75+ teams) are only partly disclosed.
- Pricing is per person contacted, not per outcome: $1.50 per person, charged daily, campaigns "from as little as $3"; user picks a pace of 5, 10 or 20 people a day ($7.50, $15 or $30 a day). Team plans (1,000+ contacts a month) are up to 40% cheaper per contact, quarterly or annual, with annual 20% cheaper than quarterly. Add-ons: $33 per warmed mailbox, $149 per GTM-engineer-managed campaign, $2,500 per month fully managed ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)). Research, lists and writing are included in the $1.50.
- Price anchoring against an eight-tool stack (Cognism/Clay, Outreach/Smartlead, Dripify/Heyreach, RB2B, Phantombuster, Common Room, ChatGPT, Heygen) ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)). Note Dripify, which the Demand AI pilot uses, is named as a replaced tool.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
Per the help centre, Ami "runs one loop" ([Meet Ami](https://help.aisdr.com/articles/6019842681-meet-ami-your-ai-gtm-engineer)):
1. **Reads the business:** website, positioning and one real email "that sounds like you". The help centre calls the sample email "the single highest leverage 5 minutes you can spend".
2. **ICP interrogation:** in a "My ICP" tab Ami suggests ICPs, asks what pain the audience has and what public evidence shows it is current, and the user likes or dislikes each idea as training signal. Good input example given: "Ops leads at 3PLs who just lost a major retail client. Evidence: layoff news or a warehouse closure in the last 90 days."
3. **Live audience:** Ami builds search queries and exclusions from live signals (hiring, funding, tech stack, news, product launches, LinkedIn activity) "rather than a static database", with waterfall enrichment across 20 providers ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)). The user can edit queries and exclusions.
4. **Copy from live research:** per-contact research for email and LinkedIn (connection requests, DMs, InMails, voice notes; AI video on Team plans).
5. **Approval gate:** "nothing goes out until you approve it"; payment is taken only at launch; "Preview Sequence" recommended before launch.
6. **Deliverability:** mailbox setup and warm-up, triple bounce checks including catch-alls, lookalike domains, rotation, domain health monitoring.
7. **Replies:** unified inbox, AI reply classification, replies "in minutes" or co-pilot mode.
8. **Repair:** Ami monitors performance and diagnoses "whether the audience or the angle broke", then rebuilds that part; the loop returns to step 2 "knowing more about who converts".
- Scoring method, signal weighting and how "who's worth chasing" is computed: Not published.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Company memory:** "Every conversation, every campaign, every correction... feeds one memory of your company"; corrections such as "agencies are off-limits" persist across campaigns ([Meet Ami](https://help.aisdr.com/articles/6019842681-meet-ami-your-ai-gtm-engineer)).
- **Tiered model routing:** Ami has its own credit balance, and spend depends on "which Ami model you use: Light, Medium, or High" ([Meet Ami](https://help.aisdr.com/articles/6019842681-meet-ami-your-ai-gtm-engineer)). Underlying model vendors: Not published.
- **Campaign corpus as prior:** the claimed moat is learning from 14K to 17K+ past campaigns. Whether this is fine-tuning, retrieval over past campaigns or prompting: Not published.
- **Disciplined experimentation:** a test "changes one variable, runs matched ICPs in each arm, uses a primary metric we set before starting, and runs for at least four weeks"; losing tests are published ([experiments FAQ](https://aisdr.com/outreach-experiments/)). Results include: dropping links and open tracking moved a campaign "from 0 meetings to 8" ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/remove-open-rate-tracking-and-links/)); LinkedIn connection notes gave "7x more replies" ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/linkedin-connection-request-results/)); AI personalisation beat templates "except in Germany" ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/cold-email-personalization-vs-templates/)); AI memes did not lift replies ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/ai-memes-cold-email-test/)); Saturday sends added no positive replies ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/cold-outreach-monday-vs-saturday/)); customer-owned domains can delay email launches "by up to 7 months" versus managed domains ([post](https://aisdr.com/blog/cold-email-domain-setup/)). Vendor claims; read via the index only.
- **Machine-readable commerce:** a plain-text pricing page "for AI agents" and an AI agent that gives the product demo ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)).
- **Integrations:** Gmail, Outlook, HubSpot (two-way sync), Salesforce (Team plans), Aircall, Calendly ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)).
- Job postings that could reveal stack: not found.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Speed to first campaign:** website plus one email, under one business day of setup, no sales call ([pricing.md](https://aisdr.com/pricing-for-llms/)).
- **Bundled deliverability:** their own data suggests domain set-up is the biggest launch delay, so owning infrastructure removes the main failure point.
- **Signal-first targeting:** their report argues failures come from "context and timing", not copy, and Ami's interrogation forces a timing reason into every campaign.
- Inference: human GTM engineers behind Team plans and $149 managed campaigns backstop agent quality.
- Low-commitment pricing lets many small campaigns feed the corpus.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Qualified lead identification:** require each segment to carry a written "pain plus public evidence" statement (for example, "plant expansion announced in last 90 days") before it can be scored as qualified.
- **Segmentation:** add like/dislike feedback on proposed micro-segments and store the reason, so the next proposal set learns the client's taste.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** when a campaign stalls, have the NBC step diagnose "audience or angle" separately and recommend changing only one.
- **Content creation:** ask for one real email from the client's best rep at onboarding and use it as the style anchor for all generated copy.
- **Campaign execution:** for the pilot, run Demand AI tests with AiSDR's rule set: one variable, matched segments, pre-set metric, four weeks minimum.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** keep a persistent "corrections memory" (exclusions, tone rules) that applies to every future campaign.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 18. Aurasell
**Website:** [aurasell.ai](https://www.aurasell.ai/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2024, San Mateo / San Francisco Bay Area, offices in Bangalore and London ([Pulse 2.0](https://pulse2.com/aurasell-30-million-seed-raised-to-launch-ai-native-crm-platform), [Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/aurasell/), third-party) | **Category:** AI-native CRM and "AI-Native GTM OS" overlay

**Differentiator:** Aurasell treats the CRM as just a structured database underneath an agentic operating system, and offers that system either as a full CRM replacement (SMB) or as an overlay on Salesforce or HubSpot (mid-market and enterprise). Its approach to scoring is to let RevOps describe their judgement to an Agent Builder, dry-run it on a real book, and add a short reasoning field to every score. It is also willing to fine-tune small open-weight models for latency and cost, and writes about it.

**Source coverage:**
Website/product pages: read ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)) | Pricing: not found on site (third-party only) | Docs/help centre: not found | Changelog: not found (blog serves as release log) | Blog: read, 2 posts in full ([Live Assist](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/introducing-live-assist), [prioritisation agents](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)) plus [index](https://www.aurasell.ai/blog) | Case studies: read (homepage outcome cards; [Oumi model case study](https://oumi.ai/blog/case-study-aurasell-outperforms-sonnet) from pass 1) | Video/YouTube: read ([CX Today interview](https://cxtoday.com/crm/legacy-crm-wasnt-built-for-ai-the-case-for-a-new-stack)) | Founder posts/podcasts: read (CX Today), search extract ([RevOps Co-op demo](https://www.revopscoop.com/revops-demos/aurasell-ai-native-crm-demo)) | Funding/press: read (pass 1 sources) | Jobs: not found (searched ATS boards and job sites) | Integrations/marketplaces: read (Salesforce, HubSpot overlay; homepage) | Investor filings: not applicable

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- $30M seed (August 2025) co-led by Next47, Menlo Ventures and Unusual Ventures (third-party) ([Pulse 2.0](https://pulse2.com/aurasell-30-million-seed-raised-to-launch-ai-native-crm-platform)).
- Founders CEO Jason Eubanks (former CRO at Harness) and CTO Srinivas Bandi ([Pulse 2.0 interview](https://pulse2.com/aurasell-profile-jason-eubanks-interview/amp/), [SaaStr](https://saastr.com/ai-app-of-the-week-aurasell-the-ai-native-crm-thats-actually-replacing-15-tools), third-party).
- Release cadence: CRM launch 26 August 2025 ([launch post](https://www.aurasell.ai/resources/aurasell-launches)); GTM OS overlay 12 February 2026 ([SiliconANGLE](https://siliconangle.com/2026/02/12/aurasell-launches-ai-native-go-market-operating-system-salesforce-hubspot/)); Agent Builder June 2026 ([Demand Gen Report](https://www.demandgenreport.com/industry-news/news-brief/aurasell-launches-agent-builder-for-go-to-market-teams/53554/)); Live Assist 13 October 2026 ([blog](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/introducing-live-assist)).
- About 30 customer logos on the homepage, including Xerox, EDB, Trading Technologies, TCI Transportation, Cyera, Lightning AI, Waabi, Fetch.ai and E2B; named executive testimonials from EDB's CEO and Lightning AI's CRO ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).

**Vendor claims**
- More than 50 customers, over $6M ARR, 37% replacing HubSpot or Salesforce, 185M+ agentic workflows run (mid-2026) ([Demand Gen Report](https://www.demandgenreport.com/industry-news/news-brief/aurasell-launches-agent-builder-for-go-to-market-teams/53554/)).
- "250+ and counting" GTM agents; database of 950M contacts and 84M accounts ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- Positioning is consolidation and productivity: "15+ tools in one platform", replacing Clay, Gong, Outreach, Nooks, Clari, ZoomInfo, 6sense, DocuSign and others in a comparison table ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).
- Homepage outcome cards (vendor claims): TCI 12.3% close-rate lift, 5+ tools eliminated, 45 days to rollout, and "Agent Builder turned what would traditionally be a multi-team RevOps project into a ten-minute self-service deployment"; Avo Automation 70 to 80% less research and prep time, 35% faster velocity; Impala AI 99% reduced sequence time, 80% lower tool spend; Accsense about 50% faster deal cycles ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).
- "Go from human to superhuman operators in under two hours" (vendor claim) ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).
- Pricing: not published. Third-party reports suggest annual contracts from about $20,000 ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/aurasell/), third-party); $99 and $199 per-seat figures appear only on an aggregator (unverified) ([aiagentrank](https://aiagentrank.io/agent/aurasell)).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Data in:** native contact and account database, native intent signals and waterfall enrichment; first-party conversations, meetings, email and web activity unified into a context graph ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/), [SiliconANGLE](https://siliconangle.com/2026/02/12/aurasell-launches-ai-native-go-market-operating-system-salesforce-hubspot/)). Customers can also bring Bombora intent and website tracking ([prioritisation post](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
2. **ICP and territory:** native ICP matching and a Territory Agent ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)).
3. **Prioritisation built by conversation:** RevOps describes "what makes you pay attention, what makes you hold off, what tells you someone is already being worked"; the Agent Builder asks about weights and timing, proposes formulas and tier thresholds, and is told to be "as deterministic as possible" ([prioritisation post](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
4. **Promotion, suppression, precedence:** intent, website visits, ICP fit and engagement promote; an open opportunity, a completed demo, or an unprospectable account suppress; precedence rules decide (for example, lower priority when an AE is already working the account even if intent is strong) ([same](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
5. **Proxies for missing events:** "a meeting that actually happened with an AE, supported by a transcript" is used as a completed-demo proxy ([same](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
6. **Dry-run and spot-check:** run on one rep's book, check tier distribution, familiar records and surprising results, then go live; separate cadences (accounts weekly, contacts daily); stale signals must decay so records move down as well as up ([same](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
7. **Explainability:** a short reasoning field added after launch, for example "Low: strong intent, but an AE is already working an opportunity" ([same](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
8. **Outreach and deal stages:** sequencer across email, LinkedIn and voice, voice dialer, call preparation, Opportunity Copilot auto-updates, MEDDPICC or "3 Whys" deal scoring, forecasting, CPQ and e-signature ([homepage](https://www.aurasell.ai/)). How sequence copy is generated: Not published.
9. **Live calls:** Live Assist detects buyer questions and returns context-aware answers mid-call (below).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Named layers:** Aura Context (graph linking conversations and signals), Aura Trust (RBAC and policy), Aura IQ ([Demand Gen Report](https://www.demandgenreport.com/industry-news/news-brief/aurasell-launches-agent-builder-for-go-to-market-teams/53554/)).
- **Live Assist architecture (published):** fuses the live conversation, the contact and account context graph, and the knowledge base. Early versions took 8 to 10 seconds; the target became under two seconds. They rejected frontier models as too slow, chose open-weight models and "fine-tuned them hard for this one specific job", and matched them to mid-tier GPUs. Pipeline: catch the question, classify intent, retrieve context and knowledge, generate. Unanswered questions are logged and fed back to fill knowledge gaps; a "readiness layer" will score what share of real buyer questions a knowledge base can answer ([Live Assist post](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/introducing-live-assist)).
- **Small model for research (published by partner):** an 8B Qwen3 model fine-tuned with Oumi on about 2,000 frontier-model summaries, evaluated by LLM judges for groundedness and coverage; reported +8% coverage and +12% groundedness versus Sonnet 4.5 ([Oumi](https://oumi.ai/blog/case-study-aurasell-outperforms-sonnet)).
- **Hybrid deterministic plus LLM scoring:** rules first, AI reasoning in final scoring, human spot-checks ([prioritisation post](https://www.aurasell.ai/blogs/account-contact-prioritization-agents)).
- **CEO thesis:** CRM should become "a structured database feeding a broader agentic go-to-market operating system" ([CX Today](https://cxtoday.com/crm/legacy-crm-wasnt-built-for-ai-the-case-for-a-new-stack)).
- Identity resolution, enrichment providers, write-back conflict rules for the overlay: Not published. Job postings: not found.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Consolidation economics** land with CFOs; results are claimed mainly as tool spend and cycle time, which are easier to show than net-new pipeline (Inference).
- **Two adoption paths** (replace or overlay) widen the funnel.
- **Self-serve agent building** moves RevOps work from weeks to minutes, per TCI.
- **Engineering depth on latency and cost** (small fine-tuned models) makes always-on agents affordable at their price point (Inference).

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** restructure our scoring into three explicit parts: promotion signals, suppression rules (open deal, existing customer, recent demo), and precedence. Show it as a table the client can read.
- **Lead scoring:** add a one-line reason to every tier ("Tier B: strong fit, no signal in 60 days") so reps can act on it.
- **Lead scoring:** add a "dry-run on one rep's accounts" step before a scoring change goes live, with tier distribution and three spot-checks.
- **Segmentation:** run account prioritisation weekly and contact prioritisation daily, as separate jobs.
- **Qualified lead identification:** document proxies explicitly when an event is not captured (for example, "RFQ email received" as a buying-stage proxy for industrial buyers).
- **Content creation:** log every question our content library could not answer and use it as the content studio backlog; a "readiness" score per segment is a cheap version.
