### 24. Bombora
**Website:** [bombora.com](https://bombora.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2014 / New York | **Category:** B2B data company (intent, identity, audiences, campaign measurement) built on a publisher and brand data co-op

**Differentiator:** Bombora is a neutral data supplier rather than a workflow platform: it collects consented content consumption through a co-op, classifies it into a customer-extendable topic taxonomy, and scores each company's surge against its own baseline. Its strategy is "usage through ubiquity", embedding the data in other vendors' tools and now in customers' agents, rather than locking it in a UI ([CEO interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/), third-party).

**Source coverage:** Website: read ([intent](https://bombora.com/intent/), [co-op](https://bombora.com/co-op/) in pass 1; [resources](https://bombora.com/resources/)) | Pricing: not published; third-party only ([Docket](https://www.docket.io/resources/research/bombora-pricing)) | Docs: read ([thresholding guide](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/thresholding)); knowledge base FAQs search extract only ([ADAT FAQ](https://knowledge.bombora.com/bombora-knowledge-base-resources/company-surge-feed-all-domains-all-topics-adat)) | Changelog: read ([customer release notes](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/releases)) | Blog: read ([co-op blog](https://bombora.com/blog/bomboras-data-co-op-persistent-strength-through-diversity-in-an-ai-driven-world/), [taxonomy guide](https://bombora.com/core-concepts/the-complete-guide-to-bomboras-topics-and-taxonomy/)) | Case studies: titles and figures only ([resources](https://bombora.com/resources/); MiQ via [CEO interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/)) | Video: titles only ([press coverage list](https://bombora.com/press-coverage/)) | Leadership: read ([Mark Connon interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/)) | Press: read ([press coverage](https://bombora.com/press-coverage/), [B2beacon](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/bombora-expands-b2beacon-establishing-a-new-standard-for-b2b-campaign-measurement-302677395.html)) | Jobs: search extract only ([Built In data engineer](https://builtin.com/job/data-engineer-i-reno-nv-hybrid-us/7125025)) | Integrations: read in pass 1 ([HubSpot](https://bombora.com/integration/hubspot/)) | Filings: not applicable (private)

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts**
- Taxonomy growth is fast and measurable: 17,210 topics (March 2025), 20,132 (February 2026), 21,632 (June 2026) and **25,378 after the "Quay" release on 28 September 2026**, which added 3,768 topics; the next release is due February 2027 and customers can submit topics (deadline 12 November 2026) ([release notes](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/releases)). This corrects pass 1, which gave about 20,100.
- Co-op of "almost 6,000 publisher and brand sites" with member retention "close to 100%" ([co-op blog](https://bombora.com/blog/bomboras-data-co-op-persistent-strength-through-diversity-in-an-ai-driven-world/)); the CEO says hundreds of sites are added each year ([interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/), third-party).
- New products: B2beacon campaign measurement, launched on The Trade Desk in Q4 2025 and extended in February 2026 via OpenX to DV360, Amazon DSP, Yahoo DSP, Viant, Basis and Simpli.fi, with "90%+" match rates and an identity graph mapping "more than 3 billion digital identifiers" ([PR Newswire](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/bombora-expands-b2beacon-establishing-a-new-standard-for-b2b-campaign-measurement-302677395.html)); predictive audiences with Proximic by Comscore (January 2026) and audiences on Reddit (December 2025) ([press coverage list](https://bombora.com/press-coverage/), titles only).
- Recognition: Leader in The Forrester Wave for intent data providers (Q1 2025) ([press release](https://bombora.com/company_press/bombora-recognized-as-a-leader-among-b2b-intent-data-providers-by-independent-research-firm/)); "Marketing Insights Platform of the Year" in the 2026 MarTech Breakthrough Awards ([PR Newswire](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/bombora-named-marketing-insights-platform-of-the-year-in-2026-martech-breakthrough-awards-302844290.html), search extract).
- Distribution: integrations named by the CEO include 6sense, Apollo, Salesforce, HubSpot, The Trade Desk, StackAdapt, LinkedIn and LiveRamp ([interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/), third-party). Company Surge reported as a standard HubSpot intent signal from August 2026 ([Digital Applied](https://www.digitalapplied.com/blog/hubspot-company-surge-bombora-intent-crm-record), third-party).
- Revenue, funding and customer count: Not published. The "Lem0n" acquisition reported by a third party remains unverified ([Docket](https://www.docket.io/resources/research/bombora-pricing), third-party).

**Vendor claims**
- MiQ campaigns using Bombora audiences and B2beacon saw average click-through up 28% and campaign site traffic up 40%; a publisher using Surge to find under-served topics grew owned page views 213% in a year ([interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/), third-party).
- 86% of co-op data is exclusive to Bombora ([intent page](https://bombora.com/intent/)).

#### 2. How they promise results
Bombora sells data and timing, not outcomes: know which companies are researching your topics before they fill in a form. Proof is data quality (consent, exclusivity, scale, match rates) and partner case studies. Pricing is not published; third parties put entry around $30,000 a year ([Docket](https://www.docket.io/resources/research/bombora-pricing), third-party); B2beacon is offered "without a subscription" through DSPs ([PR Newswire](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/bombora-expands-b2beacon-establishing-a-new-standard-for-b2b-campaign-measurement-302677395.html)).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Collect:** co-op members (publishers, brands, events, data providers) deploy Bombora's tag; engagement on open, gated and paywalled content is captured with publisher-obtained consent ([co-op page](https://bombora.com/co-op/); [privacy blog](https://bombora.com/blog/b2b-intent-data-explained-privacy-compliance/)).
2. **Filter bots:** agent and bot traffic (about 6% of page views after a 500% rise in late 2025) is excluded from Company Surge ([co-op blog](https://bombora.com/blog/bomboras-data-co-op-persistent-strength-through-diversity-in-an-ai-driven-world/)).
3. **Classify:** NLP and deep learning map each content item to topics in the taxonomy, using context to separate homonyms ([taxonomy guide](https://bombora.com/core-concepts/the-complete-guide-to-bomboras-topics-and-taxonomy/)). Similar topics are merged (124 duplicates consolidated in January 2024) ([release notes](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/releases)).
4. **Resolve identity:** a patented method fusing deterministic, behavioural and IP-to-company data, plus a model for remote workers ([interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/); [WFH feature](https://bombora.com/blog/new-ai-feature-unlocks-work-from-home-b2b-visitor-data/)).
5. **Score:** Company Surge is 0 to 100 per company per topic; 50 is average consumption and 60+ is a "statistically significant increase" versus the company's baseline ([thresholding guide](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/thresholding)). The current FAQ describes comparing the last 3 weeks of interactions with the previous 12 weeks ([ADAT FAQ](https://knowledge.bombora.com/bombora-knowledge-base-resources/company-surge-feed-all-domains-all-topics-adat), search extract); a 2017 document listed unique users, interaction-type weighting and topic relevance as inputs ([2017 PDF](https://bombora.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/07/Bombora-Company-Surge-June-2017.pdf), search extract).
6. **Threshold:** customers pick topic clusters and require a company to surge on at least 25% of the cluster's topics (e.g. 3 of 12) before acting ([thresholding guide](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/thresholding)).
7. **Deliver:** weekly feeds and integrations to CRM, MAP, ABM and ad platforms; audiences for programmatic, CTV and Reddit; B2beacon reports account and buying-group reach. Bombora does not run outreach.

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Baseline-relative scoring** so large companies do not dominate by volume. Exact formula: Not published.
- **Cluster thresholding** as a simple, explainable noise filter.
- **Customer-driven taxonomy:** a scheduled submission and release cycle lets customers add product and company names; most additions historically were company and product names rather than concepts ([release notes](https://customers.bombora.com/crc-brand/releases)).
- **Log-level measurement:** B2beacon uses platform-direct impression logs rather than tags, matched to accounts and buying groups ([PR Newswire](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/bombora-expands-b2beacon-establishing-a-new-standard-for-b2b-campaign-measurement-302677395.html)).
- **Stack (job listings):** Google Cloud (Dataflow, Pub/Sub, BigQuery, Kubernetes), Python and Airflow for content-interaction pipelines ([Built In](https://builtin.com/job/data-engineer-i-reno-nv-hybrid-us/7125025), search extract).
- **AI search risk published openly:** broad news publisher traffic fell 25% year on year in H2 2025 while specialist publishers rose 9% ([co-op blog](https://bombora.com/blog/bomboras-data-co-op-persistent-strength-through-diversity-in-an-ai-driven-world/)). Inference: early-funnel reading signals will thin as AI answers early questions.
- **Agent readiness:** the CEO says the focus is now ensuring the data "can be used by the agents that our customers are building" ([interview](https://pulse2.com/bombora-profile-mark-connon-interview/), third-party). No Bombora MCP server was found. Persona-level method: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
Exclusive, consented supply that is hard to replicate, a neutral position that lets competitors (6sense, Apollo, HubSpot) distribute it, and a taxonomy that keeps pace with new products. The trade-off is that customers must turn signals into action themselves, and the signal depends on publisher traffic that AI search is eroding.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Lead scoring:** score each signal type as change against the account's own baseline (e.g. last 3 weeks versus prior 12) rather than raw counts, with an explicit "surging" threshold.
- **Qualified lead identification:** require evidence across several related topics or signal types (a cluster threshold such as 3 of 12) before an account is flagged.
- **Segmentation:** group accounts by the topic cluster they are surging on; for industrial products, build clusters by application (e.g. pumps for water treatment) so the cluster also names the message theme.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** tag every content library asset with taxonomy topics so the first touch matches what the account is reading.
- **Prospecting:** accept Bombora-style CSV feeds (domain, topic, score) as a standard signal upload and let users add their own product terms to the topic list.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** report reach and engagement at account and buying-group level, not only per lead.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 25. Outreach
**Website:** [outreach.ai](https://www.outreach.ai/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2014 / Seattle | **Category:** sales engagement platform repositioned as an "agentic AI platform for revenue teams" (sequences, Kaia conversation intelligence, deals, forecasting, agents)

**Differentiator:** Outreach owns the execution layer (sequences, dialler, call recording) so its agents can write research into structured fields, act on reply and call content, and send through the same system reps already use. It prices AI per discrete action with published credit allowances per tier, and is now reporting AI-specific growth metrics.

**Source coverage:** Website: read ([Revenue Agent](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents/revenue-agent), [AI agents](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents) in pass 1) | Pricing: read ([pricing](https://www.outreach.ai/pricing)) | Docs: read in pass 1 ([Research Agent doc](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000425183-outreach-research-agent-overview)) | Changelog: read for April, search extract for February to September 2026 ([April notes](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000429739-outreach-product-release-notes-april-2026), blog](https://www.outreach.ai/resources/blog/explore-24-outreachs-ai-prospecting-agent)) | Case studies: read, figures ([momentum release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)) | Video: read (transcript of the Revenue Agent session on the [Revenue Agent page](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents/revenue-agent)) | Leadership: read (CEO Abhijit Mitra quote in [momentum release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)) | Press: read ([Omni launch](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260427304135/en/Outreach-Launches-Omni-Reimagining-How-Revenue-Teams-Execute-with-AI-Agents), [TEI](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/new-commissioned-total-economic-impact-study-models-244-roi-and-19-2m-in-benefits-present-value-for-composite-organization-using-outreach) search extract) | Jobs: read, 1 posting ([Senior Applied Scientist](https://jobs.lever.co/outreach/03198a0a-e455-43fd-9666-30ec5fedcb6b)) | Integrations: search extract only (Salesforce AgentExchange listing, 15 September 2026, per [newsroom search](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/category/press-releases)) | Filings: not applicable (private)

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts (first-party, unaudited)**
- First half 2026: 12x growth in AI credit consumption, 480% year-on-year AI ARR growth in Q2 of its fiscal year, Kaia engagement up 40% ([momentum release, 9 September 2026](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)). Total ARR and the AI ARR base: Not published.
- Product cadence (monthly release notes): Meeting Prep Agent beta and Omni chat (February); Agent Studio visual canvas (April); agents can create and delete accounts, opportunities and prospects (May); Personalization Agent gains web search with citations (June); Deal Agent writes to picklist, numeric and percentage fields (July); MCP connector management and Knowledge Search on mobile (August); Omni inside Slack (September) ([release notes search](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000434715-outreach-product-release-notes-august-2026), search extract; [April notes](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000429739-outreach-product-release-notes-april-2026)).
- Named customers: Databricks, SAP, Siemens, Verizon ([Omni launch](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260427304135/en/Outreach-Launches-Omni-Reimagining-How-Revenue-Teams-Execute-with-AI-Agents)).
- Background: valued at $4.4bn in 2021; 9% cut in 2024 after 12% in 2023; Abhijit Mitra replaced co-founder Manny Medina as CEO in 2024 ([GeekWire](https://www.geekwire.com/2024/outreach-cuts-9-of-workforce-in-latest-layoffs-at-seattle-sales-automation-startup/); [GeekWire](https://www.geekwire.com/2024/outreach-ceo-manny-medina-steps-down/), third-party). ARR estimated at $250M in 2023 ([Sacra](https://sacra.com/c/outreach/), third-party estimate).

**Vendor claims**
- SolarWinds' win-back agent: 45% reply rate, 100+ accounts reactivated, $200,000 pipeline re-opened; Resi: 1.4M Kaia recordings and a 35% mid-market win rate ([momentum release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)).
- Revenue Agent early adopters: 15% to 20% higher reply rates, 10x faster personalisation, 3x MQL replies, up to 50% higher meeting conversion ([Revenue Agent page](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents/revenue-agent)).
- Commissioned Forrester TEI: 244% three-year ROI, payback under six months, for a composite organisation of $9bn revenue and 600 users ([TEI release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/new-commissioned-total-economic-impact-study-models-244-roi-and-19-2m-in-benefits-present-value-for-composite-organization-using-outreach), search extract).
- "Average 26%" higher win rates ([platform page](https://www.outreach.ai/platform)); method: Not published.

#### 2. How they promise results
- Productivity and pipeline without extra headcount; tier pages attach outcome claims ("predict deal outcomes with 81% accuracy", "ramp reps 3x faster", "reduce forecast errors by 15%") ([pricing](https://www.outreach.ai/pricing)).
- **Pricing:** seats plus AI credits. Tiers: Amplify Essentials (10,000 credits, conversation intelligence), Amplify Core (25,000, pipeline creation with Revenue and Personalization Agents), Amplify Plus (50,000) and Amplify Pro (100,000, forecasting and managed services). Credits are deducted per "discrete action" such as generating a message or running a search, each with a predefined cost; extra packs can be bought; forward-deployed engineers are offered for agent deployment ([pricing](https://www.outreach.ai/pricing)). Prices: Not published. Usage-based, not outcome-based.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
From Outreach's own walkthrough of its internal Revenue Agent set-up ([Revenue Agent video transcript](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents/revenue-agent)):
1. **Target:** filter the book of accounts (e.g. those using a competing forecasting tool) and segments (enterprise, corporate).
2. **Watch for a trigger:** the agent monitors those accounts and waits for a signal such as a sales workforce change, product launch, funding round or investment.
3. **Choose personas:** e.g. director and above in sales, operations, marketing, finance; the agent sources new contacts where missing.
4. **Pick a sequence** and insert AI prompting into the email steps, mixing value propositions with account data and third-party signals.
5. **Set the sender:** existing contact owner, or account AE for newly sourced contacts.
6. **Human review:** the rep opens the drafted email in the normal task list, edits if needed and sends. Sequences can also run autonomously with hand-off on engagement ([AI agents page](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents)).

Supporting agents and data: the Research Agent extracts structured insights from Kaia transcripts, emails or Gemini-grounded web search and saves them to account fields that drive filters and triggers ([support doc](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000425183-outreach-research-agent-overview)); the Revenue Agent also draws on engagement history, third-party signals (funding, hiring, tech stack), CRM data and first-party data such as product usage via Snowflake ([Revenue Agent FAQ](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents/revenue-agent)); the Deal Agent proposes opportunity field updates from call signals ([AI agents page](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents)); the Personalization Agent grounds copy in a "Knowledge" store ([Omni launch](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260427304135/en/Outreach-Launches-Omni-Reimagining-How-Revenue-Teams-Execute-with-AI-Agents)). Previewed next: Next Best Actions built in Agent Studio from public data, past engagements and conversations; a Reply Agent that detects reply intent and drafts grounded responses ([momentum release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Research as structured fields** that become filterable and can trigger sequences; turns LLM output into workflow data.
- **Trigger-wait pattern:** agents sit on a defined account list and act only when a named signal fires, rather than mass-sending.
- **Models:** Google Gemini with Google Search grounding for external research ([support doc](https://support.outreach.io/support/solutions/articles/159000425183-outreach-research-agent-overview)); other models: Not published.
- **MCP in both directions:** Outreach MCP server and client in every tier ([pricing](https://www.outreach.ai/pricing)).
- **Admin governance:** AI Control Hub to view, enable and disable AI features ([momentum release](https://www.outreach.ai/company/newsroom/outreach-reports-12x-ai-usage-growth-as-revenue-teams-move-beyond-insight-to-ai-driven-execution)); ISO 42001 certified ([AI agents page](https://www.outreach.ai/ai-agents)).
- **Jobs:** the Dialog Understanding and Generation team turns voice streams and real-time transcription into "structured intelligence" for agentic workflows; skills include LLM architectures, text classification, entity recognition, dialog, low-latency serving; languages Python, Java, Go or C++ ([Senior Applied Scientist](https://jobs.lever.co/outreach/03198a0a-e455-43fd-9666-30ec5fedcb6b)). Inference: conversation data is the core proprietary input.
- Account-intent scoring for the Revenue Agent: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
Ownership of sending, calls and recordings gives first-party engagement and conversation data and a place where reps already work, so adoption needs no behaviour change ("their workflow does not change at all", per the transcript). Credit pricing lets usage grow inside existing seats, which explains the 12x credit growth headline. Headwinds: slower overall growth, repeated layoffs, competition from AI-native SDR tools and from CRMs adding agents; most outcome numbers are early-adopter or commissioned.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Prospecting:** store AI research outputs as named account fields (e.g. "recent plant expansion", "new capex budget") so they drive filters, segment rules and triggers.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** add a "watch and wait" mode: an account sits in a segment with a defined trigger list and the NBA fires only when a trigger appears, recording which trigger caused it.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** allow an AI prompt per sequence step that combines the step's purpose, the triggering signal and the persona, and show the rendered draft for review.
- **Campaign execution:** set the sender rule per play (account owner for known contacts, assigned rep for new ones) and export ready-to-send drafts to Dripify and email tools.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** classify reply intent and draft a grounded response from the content library; add win-back plays for closed-lost accounts, the use case with Outreach's clearest published result.
- **Deal:** keep an admin switch per AI feature so the pilot client can enable autonomy step by step.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach

### 26. Salesloft (merged with Clari)
**Website:** [salesloft.com](https://www.salesloft.com/) | **Founded / HQ:** 2011 / Atlanta; private, Vista Equity Partners majority owner ([David Cummings](https://davidcummings.org/2021/12/24/salesloft-acquired-by-vista-equity/), third-party) | **Category:** Sales engagement, conversation intelligence and forecasting, marketed as a "Predictive Revenue System"

**Differentiator:** Salesloft owns the seller's daily task queue (Cadence plus Rhythm) and, since the Clari merger, the forecast. Its distinctive mechanism is a published signal contract: any partner or customer can register a typed buyer signal with boolean "indicators", which an admin-defined Play turns into a task, and the Conductor AI "Buyer Engagement" model ranks that task with the indicator text as the explanation. Few competitors document this pipeline in such detail, and none combine it with a forecasting dataset of this size.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read ([platform overview](https://salesloft.com/platform/conductor-ai/), [AI agents](https://www.salesloft.com/platform/ai-agents), [Cadence](https://salesloft.com/platform/cadence-automation/ai-email-agents)) | Pricing: not published by vendor; third-party estimates, search extract only ([MarketBetter](https://marketbetter.ai/blog/salesloft-pricing-breakdown-2026/)) | Docs: read ([Sending Signals](https://developers.salesloft.com/docs/platform/rhythm-resources/sending-signals/), [Signals FAQ](https://developer.salesloft.com/docs/platform/rhythm-resources/signals-faq/)); help centre search extract only | Changelog: read ([October 2026 release notes](https://community.salesloft.com/product-updates/october-2026-release-notes-2865)); January to August 2026 notes search extract only ([index](https://champions.salesloft.com/product-updates/june-2026-release-notes-580)) | Blog: search extract only ([Rhythm examples](https://www.salesloft.com/resources/blog/6-inspiring-examples-of-salesloft-aes-winning-with-salesloft-rhythm/)) | Case studies: read (customer quotes on [Cadence page](https://salesloft.com/platform/cadence-automation/ai-email-agents)) | Video: search extract only ([Qualified webinar demo](https://qualified.com/plus/orchestrate-your-entire-sales-process-with-salesloft-ai); agent videos linked from [AI agents page](https://www.salesloft.com/platform/ai-agents)) | Leadership: read (CEO and CMO quotes in [rebrand release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-unveils-unified-brand-identity), CPO in [MCP release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-mcp-server-revenue-data-ai-ecosystem)) | Press: read ([rebrand](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-unveils-unified-brand-identity), [MCP](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-mcp-server-revenue-data-ai-ecosystem), [Spring 2026](https://www.salesloft.com/innovation/feature-releases/spring-2026-product-update), [1mind and Drift sunset](https://www.destinationcrm.com/Articles/CRM-News/CRM-Across-the-Wire/Clari---Salesloft-Partners-with-1mind-173786.aspx)) | Jobs: read, 1 posting ([Sr. Software Engineer, Bengaluru](https://jobs.generalcatalyst.com/companies/drift-com/jobs/87124573-staff-data-scientist-agentic-ai)); 3 more search extract only | Integrations: read (signal developer docs); marketplace search extract only ([Seismic listing](https://marketplace.salesloft.com/partners/seismic?taxon_id=8)) | Investor filings: not applicable (private)

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts:**
- Clari and Salesloft agreed to merge on 7 August 2025, citing over 5,000 organisations and customers including Adobe, IBM, 3M, Zoom and Shopify ([merger announcement](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/clari-and-salesloft-announce-merger-agreement)). The merger completed on 3 December 2025 with Steve Cox as CEO ([completion release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/clari-salesloft-merger)).
- On 2 September 2026 the company began operating as one Salesloft brand; Clari Forecast keeps its name, and Clari Copilot became Salesloft Conversation Intelligence ([rebrand release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-unveils-unified-brand-identity); [AI Agent Index](https://theaiagentindex.com/resources/newsletter/2026-09-08), third-party, search extract).
- The platform page now says "Trusted by 4,000+ customers" ([platform overview](https://salesloft.com/platform/conductor-ai/)), lower than the 5,000 cited at the merger. Inference: different counting bases or post-merger churn; not explained.
- Monthly AI releases through 2026: Clari-triggered tasks (April), MCP in Claude's directory (July), ChatGPT connector (August), knowledge quality scoring (October) ([Spring 2026](https://www.salesloft.com/innovation/feature-releases/spring-2026-product-update); [MCP release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-mcp-server-revenue-data-ai-ecosystem); [October notes](https://community.salesloft.com/product-updates/october-2026-release-notes-2865)).

**Setbacks (verified):**
- Drift breach: an attacker was in Salesloft's GitHub account between March and June 2025, pivoted to Drift's AWS environment and stole OAuth tokens used to export customer Salesforce data; Mandiant found only limited reconnaissance in the core Salesloft environment ([Infosecurity Magazine](https://www.infosecurity-magazine.com/news/salesloft-github-breach-drift/), third-party; [Google Threat Intelligence](https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/data-theft-salesforce-instances-via-salesloft-drift), third-party).
- Drift retirement: on 6 March 2026 the company announced it is sunsetting Drift and will refer (not migrate) Drift customers to 1mind under an exclusive agreement, with 1mind signals feeding Cadences and the Clari forecast ([destinationCRM](https://www.destinationcrm.com/Articles/CRM-News/CRM-Across-the-Wire/Clari---Salesloft-Partners-with-1mind-173786.aspx), third-party reprint of the release). No single shutdown date was published (search extract, [eesel](https://www.eesel.ai/blog/drift-ai), third-party).
- Revenue and growth: Not published.

**Vendor claims:** 50% less admin time and 50% higher conversion to opportunity (Forrester TEI studies commissioned by the vendors) ([brand page](https://www.salesloft.com/clari-salesloft-merger)). "Five times more qualified opportunities per month" (Fushion Management Services quote) and "biggest month of new customer acquisition ever" (MOTOR Information Systems) ([Cadence page](https://salesloft.com/platform/cadence-automation/ai-email-agents)).

#### 2. How they promise results
- Rhythm launch: 23% more meetings for the same activity ([Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20230621612183/en/Salesloft-Announces-Rhythm-Powered-by-Conductor-AI-Becomes-First-Global-Revenue-Workflow-Platform-for-Full-Cycle-Sellers), search extract); an early-access customer booked 62% more meetings with 44% fewer activities ([Salesloft blog](https://www.salesloft.com/resources/blog/the-wait-is-over-rhythm-is-here/), search extract). All vendor claims; no baselines.
- The 2026 narrative is about closing an "AI execution gap": its own benchmark says only 20.6% of US and 28.3% of UK revenue leaders have production AI delivering measurable outcomes ([rebrand release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-unveils-unified-brand-identity)).
- Pricing is quote-only, per seat; third-party estimates put Advanced near $125 to $150 and Premier $165 to $200 per user per month, with a separate dialer ([MarketBetter](https://marketbetter.ai/blog/salesloft-pricing-breakdown-2026/), search extract). MCP access reportedly needs an "Agentic" add-on ([Reply.io](https://reply.io/blog/salesloft-mcp/), third-party). Not outcome-priced.

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Signals in.** Native engagement (emails, calls, Cadence steps, meetings), CRM data, Conversation Intelligence and partner signals (G2, Seismic, Vidyard, Highspot and others) flow into a "Data and AI engine" that interprets data, prioritises signals, triggers agents and pushes next steps ([platform overview](https://salesloft.com/platform/conductor-ai/)).
2. **Signal contract.** A partner registers a signal type with a data shape, an ICU-format description, at least one boolean indicator (for example "watched more than 75 percent", "viewed 5 times within 7 days") and an attribution (person, account, user, opportunity or email content). Each send needs an idempotency key; duplicates are dropped ([Sending Signals](https://developers.salesloft.com/docs/platform/rhythm-resources/sending-signals/)).
3. **Routing to an owner.** Attribution decides which seller sees it. Account-level signals are attached to the most engaged person at the account in the last 30 days (highest Buyer Engagement Score), then to the owner's last contact ([Signals FAQ](https://developer.salesloft.com/docs/platform/rhythm-resources/signals-faq/)).
4. **Play triggers a task.** An admin Play matches one signal type plus indicators; there is a strict 1:1 play to signal relationship, so AND logic must live inside one signal. Matching creates a task whose explanation is the indicator description ("Email John because he did X") (FAQ).
5. **Ranking.** Conductor AI's Buyer Engagement model orders everything in the Rhythm Focus Zone by how engaged buyers and accounts are. A new signal only starts influencing the score after it has been sent consistently for about 4 to 6 months, because the model "will need to examine the signals and how they affect outcomes". The partner-supplied urgency field is currently ignored (FAQ). A separate Deal Priority Model ranks deal tasks ([help centre](https://help.salesloft.com/s/article/Rhythm-Focus-Zone)).
6. **Content.** Account Research and Person Research agents summarise accounts and buyers; Buyer Identification recommends stakeholders using ZoomInfo; AI Email Steps generate a fresh email each time a Cadence step runs; AI Email Tasks do the same inside Plays ([AI agents](https://www.salesloft.com/platform/ai-agents)). Cadence owners can attach up to 10 documents to an AI Knowledge Library that grounds generated emails ([November 2025 notes](https://champions.salesloft.com/product-updates/november-2025-release-notes-484), search extract).
7. **Human approval.** Reps execute the ranked queue; agents recommend and draft. Since April 2026 managers can trigger Salesloft tasks and AI emails directly from Clari Inspect and Forecast ([Spring 2026](https://www.salesloft.com/innovation/feature-releases/spring-2026-product-update)).
8. **Outward access.** The MCP Server exposes pipeline, deals, accounts, Cadence activity and call intelligence to Claude, ChatGPT, Copilot and Gemini, with audit logging and write-back promised ([MCP release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-mcp-server-revenue-data-ai-ecosystem)).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Outcome-gated signal admission.** Refusing to weight a new signal until 4 to 6 months of outcome data exist is a disciplined guard against noisy intent feeds ([Signals FAQ](https://developer.salesloft.com/docs/platform/rhythm-resources/signals-faq/)). Model type and features: Not published ("patent-pending").
- **Explainability by construction.** The "why" on each task is the human-readable indicator text written by the signal provider, not a post-hoc LLM rationale.
- **RAG with document quality gates.** October 2026 release notes say uploaded knowledge files are scored for extraction quality and thin or scanned files are flagged "before they reach the vector store" ([October notes](https://community.salesloft.com/product-updates/october-2026-release-notes-2865)). Ask AI now cites the specific part of each call.
- **Record hygiene.** Person de-duplication now falls back to first name plus last name plus phone when email is missing, and CSV imports merge matches automatically (October notes).
- **Stack from hiring.** A July 2026 Bengaluru posting asks for multi-agent orchestration, "large-scale RAG pipelines", embeddings at scale, vector search (Elasticsearch, Pinecone, Weaviate, FAISS), Kafka/SQS/Kinesis, Kubernetes on AWS, Python with PyTorch, Hugging Face, LangChain or LlamaIndex, and eval frameworks such as RAGAS with vLLM or Triton inference ([posting](https://jobs.generalcatalyst.com/companies/drift-com/jobs/87124573-staff-data-scientist-agentic-ai)). A Staff Data Scientist, Agentic AI role covers the agent execution loop, memory, planning and guardrails ([Weekday](https://jobs.weekday.works/staff-data-scientist-agentic-ai-at-salesloft-wkdymiaov1), search extract).
- **Privacy stance:** "private AI models" so customer data is "never used to train models for other customers" ([platform overview](https://salesloft.com/platform/conductor-ai/)). Which LLMs: Not published.
- **"Revenue Context" dataset** combining execution, conversations and forecasting is pitched as the moat ([MCP release](https://www.salesloft.com/company/newsroom/salesloft-mcp-server-revenue-data-ai-ecosystem)); schema and training use: Not published.
- Reply classification and deliverability infrastructure: Not published.

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Workflow ownership.** The ranked queue is where reps already work, so prioritisation changes behaviour without a new tool.
- **Ecosystem leverage.** The signal API lets dozens of partners feed Rhythm, so Salesloft gets intent breadth without owning intent data.
- **Forecast plus execution.** Clari's forecasting data gives a CRO-level reason to consolidate, and links pipeline risk directly to rep tasks.
- **Limits.** Drift trust damage and retirement, quote-only pricing, few quantified agent outcomes, and a model that needs months of history before a new signal counts; built for high-volume teams.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Qualified lead identification:** Define every uploaded signal as a typed record with a type, boolean indicators, a timestamp and an idempotency key, mirroring Salesloft's schema, so duplicate rows from repeated uploads are dropped and plays can match on indicators.
- **Lead scoring:** Keep new signal types at zero weight (shown, not scored) until enough outcomes exist to judge them; display a "learning" badge. This protects tier scores from untested feeds.
- **Segmentation:** For account-level signals, attach the action to the most engaged contact in the last 30 days, falling back to the last person contacted.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Generate the "why" line from the specific indicators that fired (for example "Downloaded spec sheet twice in 7 days"), not from free LLM text.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** Let each campaign attach a small set of approved documents (Salesloft allows 10) as grounding for step emails, and score uploads for extraction quality before they enter the library.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Add a secondary de-duplication key (first name, last name, phone or company) for contacts without email, as Salesloft did in October 2026.

#### 7. What I liked about their approach
_To be completed._

### 27. HubSpot Sales Hub (Breeze agents, Breeze Intelligence, HubSpot Credits)
**Website:** [hubspot.com](https://www.hubspot.com/products/sales) | **Founded / HQ:** 2006 / Cambridge, Massachusetts (NYSE: HUBS) | **Category:** Incumbent CRM and sales suite with embedded agents, enrichment, intent and outcome-priced credits

**Differentiator:** HubSpot is the only incumbent here that prices a prospecting agent per recommended lead and meters agents, enrichment and intent on one credit ledger with self-serve spend caps. Underneath sits a broad, mostly owned signal layer: a 200,000+ site research-intent co-operative, reverse-IP visitor intent, 40+ news signal types, conversation-derived CRM signals, and partner feeds (Bombora, UserGems), soon joined by Warmly's person-level visitor identification. All of it lands natively in a CRM that 300,000+ customers already use.

**Source coverage:**
Website: read ([Prospecting Agent page](https://www.hubspot.com/products/sales/ai-prospecting-agent) in pass 1; [Credits](https://www.hubspot.com/products/artificial-intelligence/credits)) | Pricing: read ([Credits](https://www.hubspot.com/products/artificial-intelligence/credits); [April 2026 outcome pricing](https://www.hubspot.com/company-news/hubspots-customer-agent-and-prospecting-agent-now-you-pay-when-the-task-is-complete)) | Docs: read ([Prospecting Agent KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/prospecting/use-the-prospecting-agent), [intent signals](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-intent-signals), [research intent](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-research-intent), [AI lead scores](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/scoring/build-lead-scores-with-ai)); developer docs search extract only ([MCP server component](https://developers.hubspot.com/docs/apps/developer-platform/add-features/mcp-server)) | Changelog: third-party roundup read ([MarTech UNBOUND recap](https://martech.org/hubspots-unbound-2026-the-big-announcements-and-updates/)); first-party product updates blog not read | Blog: read ([dogfooding post](https://blog.hubspot.com/sales/how-we-doubled-outbound-conversion)) | Case studies: RevenueWell via earnings call (pass 1) | Video: search extract only (Academy "What is Prospecting Agent?" video embedded in [KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/prospecting/use-the-prospecting-agent)) | Leadership: read ([CEO letter, 8-K exhibit](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1404655/000119312526414965/d183135dex991.htm)); Analyst Day remarks via third-party ([Cerbat Gem/MarketBeat](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683)) | Press: read ([Warmly via CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-acquires-warmly-to-boost-ai-sales-agents/), [billing changes via CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-rolls-out-ai-spend-caps-flexible-billing/)) | Jobs: read, 2 postings ([Principal SE, Prospecting Agent](https://www.workingnomads.com/jobs/principal-software-engineer-prospecting-agent-hubspot); [Sr ML Engineer](https://jobs.generalcatalyst.com/companies/hubspot/jobs/78929816-sr-machine-learning-engineer-i), pass 1) | Integrations: read (Apollo, ZoomInfo, Surfe, Seamless, Bombora, UserGems, Claude connector, all in KB) | Investor filings: read ([Q2 2026 8-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001404655/000119312526335148/hubs-ex99_1.htm), [Q2 call](https://ir.hubspot.com/static-files/f868efed-4a81-4a8b-b0c9-a68cde86a345), [October 2026 8-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001404655/000119312526414965/d183135d8k.htm)); Q3 2026 results not yet released at 27 October 2026

#### 1. Why they are performing well
**Verified facts:**
- Q2 2026 revenue $911.7m, up 20% (17% constant currency); 306,446 customers, up 14%; FY2026 guidance $3.678bn to $3.686bn; Q3 guidance $924m to $925m ([8-K exhibit](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001404655/000119312526335148/hubs-ex99_1.htm)). On 6 October 2026 HubSpot reaffirmed Q3 and FY guidance and announced a restructuring removing about 7% of roles (nearly 660 people), with $65m to $75m of charges, to reorganise product teams around customer outcomes rather than Hubs ([8-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001404655/000119312526414965/d183135d8k.htm); [CEO letter](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1404655/000119312526414965/d183135dex991.htm)).
- Agent adoption (Q2 call): Prospecting Agent almost 17,000 customers activated; Data Agent over 16,000; Customer Agent over 10,000 ([transcript](https://ir.hubspot.com/static-files/f868efed-4a81-4a8b-b0c9-a68cde86a345)). At the 17 September 2026 Analyst Day, the CFO said 19% of Pro+ customers used agents in August (double January), monthly agentic actions rose 3.5x and credit consumption more than doubled despite April price cuts; Breeze Assistant use among Pro+ reached 55% from 37% ([Cerbat Gem/MarketBeat](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683), third-party).
- Headwinds: Q2 net customer adds of about 7,000 against a 9,000 to 10,000 expectation, net revenue retention 102%, shares fell about 21% after hours ([transcript](https://ir.hubspot.com/static-files/f868efed-4a81-4a8b-b0c9-a68cde86a345); [Investing.com](https://www.investing.com/news/transcripts/earnings-call-transcript-hubspot-beats-q2-2026-eps-shares-sink-21-after-hours-93CH-4839534), third-party).
- Warmly: agreement to acquire announced 30 June 2026, terms undisclosed; adds person-level website intent ("identifies over half of anonymous site visitors", vendor claim), an Inbound Agent and a TAM Agent; 223 paying Warmly customers were already integrated with HubSpot ([CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-acquires-warmly-to-boost-ai-sales-agents/), third-party; [Warmly announcement](https://www.warmly.ai/p/blog/warmly-is-joining-hubspot)). Closing status: not confirmed from a primary source.

**Vendor claims:** Prospecting Agent page: "76% more qualified leads", "80% more meetings", "26% higher win rate", no baseline ([product page](https://www.hubspot.com/products/sales/ai-prospecting-agent)). RevenueWell meetings booked rose from 8% to 28% (Q2 call).

#### 2. How they promise results
- **Dogfooding proof:** HubSpot's outbound team enrolled nearly all of each rep's cold book (versus 10 to 20% worked manually); the agent found a timely reason to contact 78% of enrolled contacts; booked meetings rose more than 10x and conversion doubled against the best hand-crafted campaigns; 700+ reps now use it ([HubSpot blog](https://blog.hubspot.com/sales/how-we-doubled-outbound-conversion), vendor claim).
- **"Outcomes era" framing:** the CEO says AI only produces outcomes with business context; HubSpot claims AI with poor context performs worse than no AI ([Analyst Day](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683), third-party; [MarTech](https://martech.org/hubspots-unbound-2026-the-big-announcements-and-updates/), third-party).
- **Priced on outcomes:** Prospecting Agent 100 credits ($1) per lead; Customer Agent 50 credits per resolution; Data Agent 10 credits per response; Content Agent 1,000 credits per asset; Nurture Agent 10 credits per email; credits $10 per 1,000 ([Credits](https://www.hubspot.com/products/artificial-intelligence/credits)). Caveat: the KB says credits are used whenever the agent generates outreach for a lead, "whether the lead is recommended by the agent or manually enrolled" ([KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/prospecting/use-the-prospecting-agent)).
- **Risk reducers (15 October 2026):** default account cap up to 50% above monthly credits, feature and action-level caps, seat-to-credit swaps, packs or pay-as-you-go ([Credits](https://www.hubspot.com/products/artificial-intelligence/credits); [CMSWire](https://www.cmswire.com/digital-experience/hubspot-rolls-out-ai-spend-caps-flexible-billing/), third-party).

#### 3. How they generate their outputs
1. **Context.** Per play, the admin enters what the company sells, value proposition, pain points and target products, or auto-fills them with "Analyze website" from a URL ([KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/prospecting/use-the-prospecting-agent)). Company-wide "Growth Context" and a context-completeness score in Context Home ground all agents ([Analyst Day](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683), third-party).
2. **Audience.** The play selects company segments to monitor, personas to target, and which intent signals count; a daily cap limits new contact suggestions (KB).
3. **Signals.** Tracked companies are monitored for visitor intent (reverse IP on pages you mark), research intent, CRM signals (budget mention, deal timing, churn risk, extracted from emails, transcripts and notes), about 35 news signal types (funding, executive hiring, expansion, layoffs and more), Bombora Company Surge, email bounce risk and job changes (native plus UserGems). Tracking a company backfills 30 days of signals, custom signals 90 days ([intent signals KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-intent-signals)).
4. **Recommendation.** The agent matches companies to signals and personas and writes a **Max Recommendation Score** and a **Reason to Reach Out** onto the company record; a 9am daily digest lists drafts and recommended companies (KB). Score method: Not published.
5. **Contacts.** Existing CRM contacts, or one connected provider at a time (Apollo, ZoomInfo, Surfe or Seamless) (KB).
6. **Enrolment.** Manual, Breeze Assistant prompt, workflow action, or automatic on page viewed, form submitted, days since last engagement, or list membership (KB).
7. **Outreach.** "Agent determines steps" (adaptive emails within a maximum email count and minimum wait days) or "Configure sequence steps" (user sequence with AI "smart blocks", dynamic subjects and optional smart delays). Guardrails: preset tone or learned brand voice, contact's preferred language, permitted CTAs (meeting link, a HubSpot document with a description, a link), business days, and a send window in which "the agent will choose times with the highest open rate" (KB).
8. **Approval and sending.** "Review emails" or "Send without review"; mail goes from the contact owner's, company owner's or a single user's connected inbox. Edits to a play apply only to newly enrolled contacts (KB).
9. **Replies and handoff.** The KB does not document reply handling. Community and third-party sources say outreach stops when a contact replies, books a meeting or hits the send limit ([HubSpot Community](https://community.hubspot.com/t/prospecting-agent-approve-initial-prospecting-agent-email-then-automate-follow-ups/157303), search extract). In HubSpot's own pilot, a rep called whenever a prospect engaged ([blog](https://blog.hubspot.com/sales/how-we-doubled-outbound-conversion)).
10. **Measurement.** Performance view shows enrolments, delivered, opened, clicked, replied and meetings booked (KB).

#### 4. Technical mechanisms and interesting approaches
- **Research intent co-operative.** "Powered by HubSpot's proprietary intent ecosystem of 200,000+ websites" running the HubSpot tracking code that have opted in; topics are proprietary site categorisations, not keywords. Research level is month-over-month change: High above 160%, Moderate 111 to 160%, Mild 85 to 110%, Low below 85% ([research intent KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-research-intent)). A data network effect only a CMS and CRM vendor at this scale can build.
- **Conversation-mined CRM signals with a cool-off.** Budget and timing cues from emails, transcripts and notes only fire when contact, company and deal are associated, and similar signals are suppressed for two days ([intent signals KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-intent-signals)).
- **Lead scoring.** AI-built contact fit and engagement scores (Marketing Hub Enterprise) train on contacts that made a chosen lifecycle transition within a chosen window and output editable criteria and points ([AI scores KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/scoring/build-lead-scores-with-ai)). Intent signals can feed a company combined score. Sample minimums and retraining cadence: Not published.
- **Agent harness and routing.** The CPTO described Smart CRM, an "Aviator" agent harness that routes tasks among models, and the Growth Context graph; APIs are tiered into data, context (computed insights) and work (invoke agents) ([Analyst Day](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683), third-party). HubSpot uses proprietary and third-party models ([AI Model Cards](https://trust.hubspot.com/ai)); which models per agent: Not published.
- **Hiring signals.** The Prospecting Agent principal engineer role describes an "account-led, buying-group-aware, and trust-first" system combining "intent signals, firmographics, behavioral data, and CRM context" into "explainable" prioritisation, integrating "ML and GenAI models into deterministic workflows" ([posting](https://www.workingnomads.com/jobs/principal-software-engineer-prospecting-agent-hubspot)). An ML posting cites DSPy, vLLM and agent evaluation infrastructure ([posting](https://jobs.generalcatalyst.com/companies/hubspot/jobs/78929816-sr-machine-learning-engineer-i)).
- **Openness.** Breeze agents can call external MCP servers as tools (public beta) ([developer docs](https://developers.hubspot.com/docs/apps/developer-platform/add-features/mcp-server), search extract); the Claude connector reads individual signal events and writes notes back, logged in the audit trail ([intent signals KB](https://knowledge.hubspot.com/reports/use-intent-signals)).
- **Deliverability.** Sends through reps' connected inboxes with frequency guardrails, business-day windows and open-rate send-time choice; an email bounce risk signal exists. Domain warm-up, inbox rotation or reply classification: Not published. A third-party review notes Breeze "does not own deliverability" ([Salesgear](https://www.salesgear.io/blog/hubspot-breeze-ai-for-sales/), search extract).

#### 5. Why they get the results they get
- **Installed base and native data.** A year of first-party engagement, conversation transcripts and website tracking already sits in the CRM, so the agent starts warm and writes back natively.
- **Owned intent at scale.** The research co-operative and reverse-IP layer come from HubSpot's tracking code footprint; Warmly adds person-level identification.
- **Priced to lower risk.** Trials, per-outcome prices, caps and review mode answer buyer hesitation; 4,000+ partners carry implementation ([Analyst Day](https://www.thecerbatgem.com/?p=10395683), third-party).
- **Limits.** SMB focus, vendor-reported results, opaque scoring, brokered contact data, thin reply handling and deliverability, and an organisation mid-restructure.

#### 6. What we can learn and adopt
- **Prospecting:** Offer "Analyze website" for the client: paste a URL, extract offer, value proposition and pain points into the ICP and the content brief.
- **Qualified lead identification:** Adopt HubSpot's taxonomy of news signals relevant to industrial buyers (expansion, executive hiring, regulatory approval, product launch, funding) as named, typed triggers in the signal upload template.
- **Lead scoring:** Compute signal trend as month-over-month change in explicit bands (as HubSpot does for research levels), with a short cool-off so repeated mentions do not inflate scores; later, learn editable points from accounts that converted.
- **Segmentation:** Model segments as "plays" with audience, personas, chosen signals and a daily suggestion cap.
- **Next Best Action (NBA) and Next Best Campaign (NBC):** Store a recommendation score plus a one-line reason on each account and send a daily 9am digest of top accounts and pending drafts.
- **Content mapping to each outreach sequence step:** Let users mark which document or link the system may use as each step's call to action, with a short description, and offer a "Test on this contact" preview before activation.
- **Campaign execution:** Version plays so edits only affect newly enrolled accounts; default to review mode, then compare generated with human emails before scaling.
- **Follow-up and response tracking:** Track delivered, opened, clicked, replied and meeting booked per play, and stop a sequence automatically on reply or meeting.
- **Deal:** Show a context-completeness score so the client sees which missing data weakens recommendations.

## Cross-company synthesis

Sources for every figure below are in the company sections; section numbers are in brackets. Figures a company states about itself are vendor claims unless the section says otherwise.

### Common patterns

1. **A signal triggers a play.** The unit of work is "this event happened, so run this sequence for this persona", not a static list. Seam's plays inside Clarify (trigger, qualifier, persona rule, contacts per account, destination) (1), Unify's play graphs (6), Nooks' Outbound Plays (28), Warmly's play library (14), Outreach's trigger-and-wait agents (25), ZoomInfo's GTM Studio plays (21), HubSpot's Prospecting Agent plays (27) and Salesloft's Plays, where each play matches exactly one signal type (26).
2. **Scoring is moving from black box to readable rules written with AI help.** Common Room has an LLM draft a transparent, rules-based score from closed-won data; a person previews the score distribution and switches it on (13). Aurasell builds prioritisation from promotion, suppression and precedence rules with stale-signal decay, test-runs them on one rep's accounts and stores a reasoning field on every tier (18). Seam asks plain-language fit questions, grades A to D with an explanation, and checks the model against 45 known good, bad and random accounts before scoring the full list (1). 6sense shows the top contributing signals behind each qualified account (23). This is the same shape as Demand AI's rules engine.
3. **Every claim carries its evidence.** kōdlo assembles citations in code from retrieved passages, so the model cannot invent them (5). Leadsforge attaches evidence to each signal match (4 and 16). Lightfield gives one next step with cited evidence (7). ZoomInfo returns a confidence score and lineage per record (21). Unify shows which provider supplied each field (6).
4. **Data is rented and stacked; only incumbents own it.** AI-native players run enrichment waterfalls: Leadsforge 20+ providers, Unify 40+ vendors, Rox 15 named providers with customer keys allowed, AiSDR 20 providers (4 and 16, 6, 15, 17). Incumbents own the dataset: ZoomInfo's graph, Apollo's contributor network refreshed by bounces and corrections, Bombora's co-op of about 6,000 sites, HubSpot's research-intent co-operative of 200,000+ sites, and 6sense's intent network (21, 20, 24, 27, 23).
5. **The record writes itself.** Clarify, Reevo, Lightfield, Rox, Monaco and Aurasell build the account record from email, calendar and calls instead of rep data entry (1, 2, 7, 15, 10, 18). Monaco joins objection, response and outcome at the moment of capture (10).
6. **Deterministic skeleton, AI for the wording.** Salesforce's Agent Script runs fixed steps and uses the model only where judgement or language is needed (22). nRev sells fixed node graphs with idempotent runs and a generator plus verifier model (3). Nex compiles repetitive steps into code to cut cost per run (8). ZoomInfo's Agent Teams recompute the next action from the record's current state (21). Workvivo pairs a rules engine with AI-drafted journeys (11).
7. **Human review is targeted by risk and confidence.** Nooks has its agents state a calibrated confidence so reviewers check only uncertain items (28). 6sense job postings describe risk-tiered approval levels (23). Salesforce requires approval on customer-facing actions (22). kōdlo uses approval cards before anything is imported or sent (5). nRev shows the planned workflow for approval before it runs (3).
8. **Replies are classified into actions, not just counted.** Conversica uses six action statuses tied to a rep queue and marks a lead "At Risk" if the rep did not connect after hand-off (12). Unify measures its reply classifier on weighted accuracy and pauses on out-of-office (6). Rox, 6sense and Outreach classify and route replies (15, 23, 25). AiSDR diagnoses whether a stalled campaign failed on audience or on angle (17).
9. **Decisions are logged so the system can learn.** Warmly's Context Graph stores signal, action, reason and outcome per account (14). Lightfield stores goal, audience, message and outcome per sequence (7). Nooks learns a writing style per rep from their edits and backtests strategies against historical outcomes (28). Salesloft does not weight a new signal until it has four to six months of outcome data (26). 6sense trains each customer's qualification model on their own wins (23).
10. **The leaders publish how they test their AI.** Rox runs about 200 sandbox scenarios graded by heuristics and an LLM judge (15). Unify publishes weighted-accuracy evals and the model swaps they drove (6). Nooks built a sales benchmark (28). ZoomInfo published GTM Bench, which penalises confident errors (21). AiSDR publishes its experiments, losing ones included (17).
11. **Consolidation and agent distribution.** Zoom agreed to buy Common Room and HubSpot agreed to buy Warmly in mid-2026 (closing not confirmed); Clarify bought Seam; Salesloft merged with Clari; ZoomInfo bought DoubleO.ai; Nooks bought FullyRamped; Drift customers were referred to 1mind (13, 14, 1, 26, 21, 28, 9). Most players now expose data or agents through MCP so other assistants can call them (1, 4 and 16, 6, 13, 14, 15, 20, 21, 23, 26, 27).

### How the best players promise and prove results

**What they promise.** The strongest claims are stated in the buyer's outcome unit:
- Meetings per contacts reached: AiSDR "1 to 3 qualified meetings per 100 leads" (17). Apollo's commissioned test reports 2.37% cold-to-meeting conversion against a 0.5 to 1.5% range (20).
- Pipeline in money: Unify's Perplexity case study, $1.7M pipeline in three months (6). Salesforce's own sales team, 3,200 opportunities from previously untouched leads in four months (22).
- Revenue measured in the customer's system: 1mind's HubSpot case study, influenced purchases up 25% "measured by HubSpot" (9).
- Before and after on one metric: HubSpot's own outbound team, booked meetings up more than 10x against its best hand-built campaigns (27). Nooks claims 3x meetings and 2x replies (28).
- A counterfactual: 6sense compares qualified accounts with all others each quarter and reports 29% higher opportunity value and 33% larger deals on qualified accounts (vendor-measured, 23).

**How they price around outcomes.** HubSpot charges $1 per lead its Prospecting Agent recommends, with spend caps (27). Leadsforge charges only for verified results (4 and 16). AiSDR prices per person contacted (17). Rox meters agent actions (15). Salesforce charges per agent action through Flex Credits (22). Outreach and Apollo moved to seats plus AI credits (25, 20). Seat pricing is giving way to credits and outcome meters, even among incumbents.

**How they prove it.** Proof quality varies widely:
- Strongest: audited filings (Salesforce, ZoomInfo, HubSpot), results measured in the customer's own system (1mind), and counterfactual comparisons (6sense).
- Medium: named case studies with numbers (Unify, Nooks, Apollo, Nex's single audit anecdote is weaker), and reporting the customer can edit, such as Rox's Value app with adjustable assumptions (15).
- Weakest: commissioned studies (Salesloft and Outreach Forrester TEI), logo walls without metrics (Reevo), and early companies with no published results (kōdlo, Nex).

**Lesson for us.** Pick one outcome unit, show a before and after or a counterfactual on it, let the client's own CRM be the referee, and let the client see and adjust the assumptions.

### What makes the leaders different

- **They own one wedge first, then expand.** Nooks earned the right to build agents through a dialer reps use every day (28). Warmly started with named website visitors (14). Leadsforge started with search (4 and 16). Common Room started with community signals (13).
- **They make the reason visible.** Ranking with a written "why" is now standard among leaders: HubSpot's Reason to Reach Out, Salesloft Rhythm, 6sense, Monaco, Rox (27, 26, 23, 10, 15).
- **They treat reliability as the product.** Determinism, evals, confidence flags and approval gates appear in every serious player's material. Salesforce's investment in Agent Script and production scorers suggests reliability, not raw intelligence, is the bottleneck (22).
- **They close the loop.** The best capture what happened after the action and feed it back into targeting (Warmly, Monaco, Nooks, 6sense, Salesloft).
- **They know who they are not for.** Salesforge says its AI SDR is a poor fit when deal sizes are six to seven figures, for long RFP cycles and for Fortune 500 targets (4 and 16). Most volume AI SDRs are built for short-cycle, high-volume outbound.

### Top five changes to make to Demand AI

1. **Make scoring auditable and back-tested (Lead scoring).** Let an LLM draft rule weights from the client's own won and lost accounts. Back-test the rules on a small labelled set before scoring the full list, preview the tier distribution, and store a written reason on every tier. Add promotion, suppression and precedence rules and a stacked-signal count. Learned from Common Room (13), Aurasell (18), Seam (1), Unify (6), 6sense (23). Difficulty: Low. It extends the rules engine we already have.
2. **Turn Next Best Campaign into a signal-to-play library (NBA and NBC, Content mapping).** Define each play as trigger, qualifier, persona rule, contacts per account, steps with timings, one asset per persona per step, and a "why this angle" line linking signal, angle and proof asset. Version plays so edits apply only to new enrolments. Learned from Seam (1), Salesloft (26), HubSpot (27), Nooks (28), kōdlo (5), Workvivo Journeys (11). Difficulty: Low.
3. **Close the reply loop (Follow-up and response tracking).** Bring Dripify and email replies back into Demand AI and classify each into an action status (for example: interested, not now, wrong person, out of office, unsubscribe, question). Pause the sequence on a reply, mark a lead "At Risk" when a hand-off is not followed up, and, when a play stalls, diagnose whether the audience or the angle failed. Learned from Conversica (12), Unify (6), Rox (15), AiSDR (17). Difficulty: Medium. Classification is simple; getting replies out of Dripify and inboxes is the work.
4. **Log decisions and outcomes, and report results the way the leaders do (Deal).** For every account, store the signal, the action taken, the reason and the outcome. Attribute meetings and pipeline to the play that sourced them, and report a counterfactual: accounts the tool prioritised against the rest. Learned from Warmly (14), Monaco (10), Clarify (1), 6sense (23), Rox (15). Difficulty: Low to Medium.
5. **Show evidence and confidence, and review only what is uncertain (Qualified lead identification, NBA and NBC).** Attach the source and date to every signal and research claim, assemble citations in code, and have each recommendation carry a confidence level so reviewers spend time only on uncertain items. Keep a small test set of accounts with known answers and re-run it whenever rules or prompts change. Learned from kōdlo (5), ZoomInfo (21), Unify (6), Nooks (28), Rox (15). Difficulty: Low to Medium.

Also worth doing once the five are in place: an enrichment waterfall that looks for a verified contact in the primary persona before release (Leadsforge, Unify, Apollo, ZoomInfo), and buying-committee mapping per account (Common Room's department-by-seniority grid, 13).

## Technical mechanisms by funnel stage

"Published" means the company describes the mechanism; how well it works is usually not published. Difficulty is for a small team that already has Demand AI's rules engine.

### Prospecting

| Mechanism | Who uses it | Published? | Difficulty for us |
|---|---|---|---|
| Multi-provider enrichment waterfall, with the provider shown per field and pay on success | Unify, Leadsforge, AiSDR, Rox (15 named providers), Common Room, ZoomInfo | Published; provider order mostly not | Medium: vendor contracts and per-record cost control |
| Contributory data network refreshed by send outcomes (bounces and corrections mark data stale) | Apollo, ZoomInfo | Published (Apollo via job posting) | High for the network; Low to log our own outcomes per contact |
| Public-source signal mining: tenders, patents, company registries, conference lists | kōdlo | Published (sources listed) | Medium: a scraper or search API per source |
| Hiring signals from job boards mapped to buying intent | nRev, Leadsforge, Seam | Published | Low |
| Account discovery from patterns in won, lost and stalled deals, plus a weekly lookalike job | Lightfield | Published (demo write-up) | Medium: needs deal history |
| Vector matching of public news to each user's interests (title and body embedded separately) | Rox | Published | Medium |
| Free search, paid enrichment, cached per record per year | ZoomInfo | Published | Low |
| Partner data queried at run time through one gateway | Salesforce (Data Vendor Gateway) | Published | Medium |

|---|---|---|---|

| ICP interrogation that demands "pain plus public evidence" before a campaign is built | AiSDR | Published | Low |
| Custom signals copied from the best reps' manual research | Nooks | Published (case study) | Low |

| Per-customer qualification model trained on closed-won outcomes, with top contributing signals | 6sense (6QA) | Published; model details not | Medium: needs outcome history |

---

## AI-native vs incumbent

### What the AI-native players do differently

- **Capture instead of entry.** Clarify, Reevo, Lightfield, Rox, Monaco and Aurasell build the record from conversations. Incumbent CRMs still depend on reps typing.
- **Act, then hand off.** AiSDR, Conversica, 1mind and Monaco run work end to end and hand off to people at defined points. Incumbents mostly rank and suggest, although Agentforce (Hunter is in pilot), HubSpot's Prospecting Agent and Outreach's agents are closing the gap.
- **Readable, AI-authored rules.** Common Room and Aurasell use AI to write transparent rules that a person can check, where incumbents such as HubSpot do not publish their scoring method.
- **Publish engineering and evals.** Rox, Unify, Nooks, Monaco, Aurasell and nRev explain how they build and test. Among incumbents only ZoomInfo (GTM Bench) and Salesforce (Agent Script, scorers) publish comparable detail.
- **Price on usage or outcomes.** Credits, per-result and per-action pricing are the AI-native default. HubSpot has copied it with $1 per recommended lead and spend caps.
- **Speed to value.** Free tiers and script installs (Warmly), a pre-built total addressable market on day one (Monaco), a campaign from a website and one sample email (AiSDR).
- **Bundle people.** Monaco's forward-deployed AE, nRev's GTM-engineer hours, AiSDR's managed service and Nooks' forward-deployed engineers sell an outcome rather than a tool.

### Where incumbents still win

- **Data.** ZoomInfo's graph, Apollo's self-refreshing contributor network, Bombora's exclusive consented co-op, 6sense's intent network and HubSpot's 200,000+ site research co-op. Clarify had to buy Seam for signals, and HubSpot is buying Warmly.
- **Distribution.** HubSpot switched agents on inside existing seats for almost 17,000 Prospecting Agent customers. Salesforce reports Agentforce ARR above $1.5bn, on a definition widened in Q2 FY27 to include Slackbot and Headless 360.
- **Trust and governance.** Salesforce's Trust Layer and approval rules, ZoomInfo's confidence and lineage, and 6sense's risk-tiered approvals. The Drift breach at Salesloft shows the other side: a wide integration surface carries risk.
- **Outcome history.** 6sense and Salesloft can train on years of customer outcomes; a new entrant cannot.
- **System of record.** Lightfield, Aurasell and Nex offer sync or overlay modes, which concedes that the incumbent keeps the record during any transition.

### Where incumbents are under pressure

ZoomInfo's net revenue retention is 89% and it cut about 20% of staff; HubSpot restructured about 7% of staff in October 2026; Outreach reports AI growth only as multiples without a base (21, 27, 25). Bombora reports that AI search is reducing broad publisher traffic, which thins top-of-funnel intent for every intent-based score (24).

### Gaps a new entrant like us can exploit

1. **High-ticket, long-cycle selling is underserved.** Salesforge says its AI SDR does not fit six- or seven-figure deals or long RFP cycles. HubSpot is built for SMB and mid-market volume. kōdlo targets high-ticket technical sales but is pre-traction. Clarify's FutureFit case covers 12 to 18 month cycles, which shows demand exists.
2. **Domain signals the horizontal vendors do not hold.** Installed-base end-of-support dates, service renewals, capital projects and turnaround timing come from the client's own records, not from intent co-ops. Inference: as AI search thins third-party intent, first-party and late-stage signals become more valuable, which plays to Demand AI's signal set.
3. **Transparent scoring is becoming expected.** Common Room and Aurasell show buyers want readable scores. Our rules engine already exposes every weight, decay and knock-out; the gap is AI-assisted drafting and back-testing (change 1 above).
4. **Regional data coverage.** Third-party practitioner reviews report weaker Apollo accuracy outside North America (20). Inference: a research-led approach with cited public sources (kōdlo style) may beat a bigger database for regional, technical buyers.
5. **Work on top of what clients already use.** Clients keep their CRM, Dripify and inboxes. An overlay that reads replies and writes outcomes back, like nRev and Nex, is easier to adopt than a CRM replacement.
